- [Provision PostgreSQL from VS Code](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/provision-postgresql-from-vs-code-franck-pachot-q4ede) - 2026-06-15 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [linkedin/articles/q4ede.json](linkedin/articles/q4ede.json)
  Evidence note: Walks through using Microsoft's PostgreSQL extension for VS Code to create connections and provision local Docker or managed cloud PostgreSQL instances without leaving the editor.
- [HorizonDB preview: automate a reproducible lab with ARM](https://dev.to/franckpachot/horizondb-preview-automate-a-reproducible-lab-with-arm-2515) - 2026-06-12 | Dev.to | Azure HorizonDB, PostgreSQL | High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3883285.json](devto/articles/3883285.json)
  Evidence note: An Azure Resource Manager template, deployed via the Azure CLI after az login, automates spinning up and tearing down a reproducible HorizonDB preview lab without manual portal clicks.
- [PostgreSQL Average Active Sessions Dashboard in VS Code](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/postgresql-average-active-sessions-dashboard-vs-code-franck-pachot-u1kre) - 2026-06-11 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Migration & Compatibility, Security; archive: [linkedin/articles/u1kre.json](linkedin/articles/u1kre.json)
  Evidence note: Uses the PostgreSQL VS Code extension's performance dashboard to visualize average active sessions, wait types, ranked wait events, blockers, and pg_stat_activity details during pgbench contention.
- [RUM—Storing More in the Index](https://dev.to/franckpachot/rum-storing-more-in-the-index-4hoe) - 2026-06-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/3804307.json](devto/articles/3804307.json)
  Evidence note: RUM, built by Alexander Korotkov and colleagues at Postgres Professional, extends each GIN posting-list entry with an extra datum alongside the TID to fix full-text ranking and ordering performance.
- [GIN: Understanding PostgreSQL's Inverted Index and Its Limitations](https://dev.to/franckpachot/gin-understanding-postgresqls-inverted-index-and-its-limitations-40hn) - 2026-06-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3801984.json](devto/articles/3801984.json)
  Evidence note: GIN decomposes arrays, JSONB, and tsvector values into an entry B-tree of keys pointing to posting lists of heap TIDs, saving space but leaving range queries and sorting unsupported.
- [Extended RUM in DocumentDB extension for PostgreSQL: Efficient ESR (Equality, Sort, Range) Queries](https://dev.to/franckpachot/extended-rum-in-documentdb-extension-for-postgresql-efficient-esr-equality-sort-range-queries-5bkj) - 2026-06-07 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3694387.json](devto/articles/3694387.json)
  Evidence note: A year after finding RUM indexes couldn't replace MongoDB compound indexes for sorted queries, the May 2026 DocumentDB extension's Extended RUM index preserves key ordering for pagination.
- [Getting Started with pg_durable: Workflows Inside PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/franckpachot/getting-started-with-pgdurable-durable-workflows-inside-postgresql-3980) - 2026-06-05 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3817821.json](devto/articles/3817821.json)
  Evidence note: Building the pg_durable extension from source with Rust and pgrx on PostgreSQL 17 shows how it orchestrates crash-resilient, SQL-defined workflows for ETL and long-running background jobs.
- [Filter on Children, Sort by Parent: One-to-Many Compound Index Strategies in PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/franckpachot/filter-on-children-sort-by-parent-one-to-many-compound-index-strategies-in-postgresql-1clj) - 2026-06-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3684071.json](devto/articles/3684071.json)
  Evidence note: Approximating a MongoDB compound index over embedded children in PostgreSQL requires denormalizing onto the parent or child row and maintaining consistency with cascading foreign keys or triggers.
- [BSON and OSON: documents are designed to be nested, not flat](https://dev.to/franckpachot/bson-and-oson-documents-are-designed-to-be-nested-not-flat-3b65) - 2026-05-30 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, JSON & Documents, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/3780540.json](devto/articles/3780540.json)
  Evidence note: Critiquing Oracle's YCSB and flat-document OSON benchmarks against BSON, it argues thousands of nested fields per document are acceptable in a document model, unlike flat SQL tables.
- [$exists and non-sparse indexes in MongoDB and in other DocumentDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/exists-and-non-sparse-indexes-in-mongodb-and-in-other-documentdb-19e3) - 2026-05-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3702272.json](devto/articles/3702272.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB indexes store null as a stand-in for a missing field, so a regular index scan can't distinguish $exists:false from an explicit null; only a partial index preserves that distinction.
- [The Autovacuum Scale Factor Problem at Scale - Know Your Defaults](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-autovacuum-scale-factor-problem-at-scale-know-your-defaults-5a9o) - 2026-05-26 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3716806.json](devto/articles/3716806.json)
  Evidence note: PostgreSQL's autovacuum threshold combines a fixed row count with a scale-factor percentage of table size, so tables with lots of cold, static data get inflated thresholds that delay maintenance.
- [Avoiding range overlaps in PostgreSQL with EXCLUDE constraint, better than serializable or assertions](https://dev.to/franckpachot/postgresql-exclude-constraints-for-better-concurrency-than-serializable-pob) - 2026-05-15 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3668423.json](devto/articles/3668423.json)
  Evidence note: An EXCLUDE constraint with the range overlap operator stops two seat-range bookings like 5-8 and 7-9 from colliding, enforcing the invariant without serializable isolation or SQL assertions.
- [PostgreSQL’s "random_page_cost" isn’t just about disk latency — and the default is often right when your indexes are](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/postgresqls-randompagecost-isnt-just-disk-latency-default-pachot-fphre) - 2026-05-14 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage; archive: [linkedin/articles/fphre.json](linkedin/articles/fphre.json)
  Evidence note: Explains that PostgreSQL random_page_cost models cache behavior and CPU work as well as storage latency, so lowering it merely because disks are SSDs can produce worse plans.
- [You need foreign keys and surrogate keys because you broke your relationships](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/you-need-foreign-keys-surrogate-because-broke-your-franck-pachot-wh0ne) - 2026-05-11 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [linkedin/articles/wh0ne.json](linkedin/articles/wh0ne.json)
  Evidence note: Explains how normalization decomposes aggregates and thereby creates the need for surrogate keys, foreign keys, joins, and wider transactions that embedded document structures avoid within an aggregate.
- [Do you need foreign keys and surrogate keys because you broke your relationships ?](https://dev.to/franckpachot/you-need-foreign-keys-and-surrogate-keys-because-you-broke-your-relationships-3b0j) - 2026-05-09 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3641131.json](devto/articles/3641131.json)
  Evidence note: Argues that normalizing a domain model into separate tables is what creates the need for foreign keys, reversing the common belief that related data itself requires a relational schema.
- [Codd's Connection Trap and Oracle's JOIN TO ONE](https://dev.to/franckpachot/codds-connection-trap-and-oracles-join-to-one-292f) - 2026-05-02 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3601574.json](devto/articles/3601574.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Database 26ai's JOIN TO ONE clause structurally catches Codd's 1970 connection-trap error, where joining suppliers-parts and parts-projects through a shared attribute fabricates spurious facts.
- [I need a database. What should I use?](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/i-need-database-what-should-use-franck-pachot-fdw3e) - 2026-04-29 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [linkedin/articles/fdw3e.json](linkedin/articles/fdw3e.json)
  Evidence note: Examines how Claude recommends a database, exposing how requirements, prompt framing, training sources, and familiar defaults can bias an apparently reasoned technology choice.
- [Anti-Join in MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/anti-join-in-mongodb-23eo) - 2026-04-25 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3549342.json](devto/articles/3549342.json)
  Evidence note: A PostgreSQL NOT EXISTS subquery finding users with no paid transactions compiles into a Nested Loop Anti Join plan that short-circuits as soon as one matching transaction row is found.
- [Mutable BSON and Oracle OSON](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mutable-bson-and-oracle-oson-2o04) - 2026-04-18 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3520847.json](devto/articles/3520847.json)
  Evidence note: Countering an Oracle marketing claim of a '529x' OSON advantage, it clarifies that raw BSON, like protobuf, should instead be compared to MongoDB's in-memory Mutable BSON document structure.
- [The origins of MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-origins-of-mongodb-557p) - 2026-04-07 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3465690.json](devto/articles/3465690.json)
  Evidence note: Internet Archive snapshots of 10gen.com from 2008 show MongoDB originally described as an object-oriented DBMS layer of a larger platform, before the terminology shifted to document-oriented.
- [MongoDB Query Planner](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-query-planner-io2) - 2026-04-07 | Dev.to | MongoDB, Amazon DynamoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3463621.json](devto/articles/3463621.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike DynamoDB or Redis, which drop query planning entirely, MongoDB keeps an empirical multi-planner that trial-runs candidate indexes and reuses the winning plan until it stops being optimal.
- [Single-Cluster Duality View 🃏](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-single-duality-view-pattern-can-sqljson-preserve-aggregate-locality-4ifd) - 2026-04-01 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB, MySQL, Amazon DynamoDB | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3343268.json](devto/articles/3343268.json)
  Evidence note: Proposes a Single-Cluster Duality View that keeps data physically together like MongoDB's Single Collection Pattern, unlike DynamoDB single-table design or Oracle/MySQL duality views spanning blocks.
- [Read Concern "snapshot" for snapshot isolation outside explicit transactions](https://dev.to/franckpachot/read-concern-snapshot-for-snapshot-isolation-outside-explicit-transactions-d5p) - 2026-03-30 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3431931.json](devto/articles/3431931.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB's snapshot read concern lets a multi-document scan read a consistent point-in-time view without an explicit transaction, avoiding the newer-majority-snapshot drift possible under majority.
- [SQL Assertions, ANSI join, and ORA-08697](https://dev.to/franckpachot/sql-assertions-ansi-join-and-ora-08697-3bl9) - 2026-03-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/3424907.json](devto/articles/3424907.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle AI Database 26ai's new SQL assertions correctly block two concurrent salary-plus-commission updates from exceeding 1150 by waiting on the other transaction, but only without ANSI join syntax.
- [Consistency boundaries in SQL databases vs. MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/consistency-boundaries-in-sql-vs-mongodb-54dm) - 2026-03-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3417259.json](devto/articles/3417259.json)
  Evidence note: Debunks two myths: MongoDB supports ACID transactions with a serializable document boundary even when sharded, while no SQL database fully abstracts consistency boundaries across columns and ranges.
- [Non-First Normal Forms and MongoDB: an alternative to 4NF to address 3NF anomalies](https://dev.to/franckpachot/non-first-normal-forms-1nf-and-mongodb-an-alternative-to-4nf-to-address-3nf-anomalies-17i8) - 2026-03-25 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3400093.json](devto/articles/3400093.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB's aggregation operators $unwind, $group, and $lookup implement the abstract algebra from a 1986 paper on non-first-normal-form relational databases, keeping nested data unflattened.
- [MongoDB Transaction Performance](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-transaction-performance-4dc7) - 2026-03-24 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3397246.json](devto/articles/3397246.json)
  Evidence note: Single-document writes in MongoDB already use internal WriteUnitOfWork/RecoveryUnit ACID transactions, so multi-document transactions add memory tracking but aren't uniformly slower.
- [Updating "denormalized" aggregates with "duplicates": MongoDB vs. PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/franckpachot/updating-denormalized-aggregates-with-duplicates-mongodb-vs-postgresql-3bi1) - 2026-03-03 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3298952.json](devto/articles/3298952.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB's updateMany with $set and arrayFilters lets DDD aggregates update duplicated array fields in place, while PostgreSQL JSONB updates typically require rewriting the entire document.
- [PostgreSQL global statistics on partitionned table require a manual ANALYZE](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/postgresql-global-statistics-on-partitionned-table-require-a-manual-analyze-473h) - 2026-03-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3295687.json](devto/articles/3295687.json)
  Evidence note: Because PostgreSQL auto-analyze skips the empty parent of a partitioned table, a four-year history table demo shows the planner deriving global distinct-value estimates between per-partition bounds.
- [From Relational Algebra to Document Semantics](https://dev.to/franckpachot/from-relational-algebra-to-document-semantics-b00) - 2026-02-27 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3288660.json](devto/articles/3288660.json)
  Evidence note: Relational algebra requires closure and first-normal-form value atomicity to make query equivalence mathematically derivable, while MongoDB's document model instead embeds ownership directly in shape.
- [Read‑your‑writes on replicas: PostgreSQL WAIT FOR LSN and MongoDB Causal Consistency](https://dev.to/franckpachot/read-your-writes-on-replicas-postgresql-wait-for-lsn-and-mongodb-causal-consistency-4he2) - 2026-02-21 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/3150629.json](devto/articles/3150629.json)
  Evidence note: PostgreSQL 19's WAIT FOR LSN and MongoDB's afterClusterTime causal-consistency read concern both let an app coordinate a later read to see an earlier write on a replica.
- [Top-K queries with MongoDB search indexes (BM25)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/top-k-queries-with-mongodb-search-indexes-bm25-3a41) - 2026-02-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents, AI & Search, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/3267532.json](devto/articles/3267532.json)
  Evidence note: Reproducing ParadeDB's pg_search benchmark showing GIN falling short of BM25 top-k queries, MongoDB's Lucene-based search indexes solve the same ranking problem across several deployments.
- [Relational composition and Codd's "connection trap" in PostgreSQL and MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/relational-composition-and-codds-connection-trap-in-postgresql-and-mongodb-4k34) - 2026-02-16 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization; archive: [devto/articles/3258511.json](devto/articles/3258511.json)
  Evidence note: Joining a supplier-parts table with a parts-projects table through the shared part illustrates Codd's 1970 connection trap: the join produces plausible supplier-project pairs never asserted as facts.
- [Databases after Microservices: PostgreSQL or MongoDB in a Domain‑Driven Design?](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/databases-after-microservices-postgresql-mongodb-design-franck-pachot-f5hme) - 2026-02-16 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, AI & Search; archive: [linkedin/articles/f5hme.json](linkedin/articles/f5hme.json)
  Evidence note: Compares PostgreSQL and MongoDB for domain-driven microservices, focusing on aggregate boundaries, schema ownership, consistency, joins, and whether each service should map or directly persist its domain model.
- [Cartesian product (CROSS JOIN) in MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/cross-join-in-mongodb-ep7) - 2026-02-14 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/3255209.json](devto/articles/3255209.json)
  Evidence note: Modeling clothing sizes and gender-specific fits as two separate collections shows how $lookup or write-time embedding in MongoDB reproduces a SQL CROSS JOIN's every-combination cartesian product.
- [Prisma + MongoDB “Hello World”](https://dev.to/franckpachot/prisma-mongodb-hello-world-928) - 2026-02-11 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/3235519.json](devto/articles/3235519.json)
  Evidence note: A minimal Docker setup starts MongoDB as a single-node replica set, required because Prisma's Object Document Mapper relies on MongoDB sessions and transactional behavior unavailable standalone.
- [{ w: 1 } Asynchronous Writes and Conflict Resolution in MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/w1-asynchronous-write-and-conflict-resolution-in-mongodb-non-default-5677) - 2026-02-10 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Indexing, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/3196423.json](devto/articles/3196423.json)
  Evidence note: A Raft-inspired consensus elects each shard's primary and replicates its oplog to secondaries, letting MongoDB keep serving consistent reads and writes to a majority even during a network partition.
- [Normal Forms and MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/normal-forms-and-the-document-model-mongodb-19f9) - 2026-02-07 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3239858.json](devto/articles/3239858.json)
  Evidence note: A pizzeria example starting from one manager, one variety, and one area shows normal-form dependencies still apply to MongoDB documents, though the model allows more flexibility applying them.
- [Serializable Transactions in MongoDB: The Doctor's On-Call Shift example](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-doctors-on-call-shift-with-snapshot-isolation-in-mongodb-18nn) - 2026-02-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3237660.json](devto/articles/3237660.json)
  Evidence note: Because MongoDB lacks explicit locking and serializable isolation, an atomic update to doctors' on-call flags together, in optimistic-concurrency style, prevents the write-skew anomaly.
- [The Doctor's On-Call Shift solved with SQL Assertions](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-doctors-on-call-shift-solved-with-sql-assertions-30fh) - 2026-02-01 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, YugabyteDB | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3214175.json](devto/articles/3214175.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle AI Database 26ai's SQL assertions enforce 'at least one doctor on call per shift' as a table-level invariant, a cross-row condition standard SQL CHECK constraints cannot express.
- [{ 🌱: "One year !"}](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/one-year-franck-pachot-zki0e) - 2026-01-28 | LinkedIn | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [linkedin/articles/zki0e.json](linkedin/articles/zki0e.json)
  Evidence note: Reflects on a first year at MongoDB after a SQL-focused career, connecting document modeling, distributed systems, storage-engine learning, and independent evidence-based developer advocacy.

  Evidence note: Installing CloudNativePG 2.18's Kubernetes operator introduces Cluster, Pooler, Backup, and Publication/Subscription resources that automate PostgreSQL failover, unlike unmanaged replication.
- [Anti-Pattern: Read Replicas Without Sharding](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/anti-pattern-read-replicas-without-sharding-franck-pachot-f4tke) - 2026-01-24 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Events & Community; archive: [linkedin/articles/f4tke.json](linkedin/articles/f4tke.json)

  Evidence note: BSON is a general-purpose Protobuf-like transport and storage format, while Oracle's OSON embeds metadata so the database can query and navigate JSON fields without a document-database smart driver.

  Evidence note: Among five Java classes implementing the BSON interface, the driver-recommended Document class balances flexibility and ease of use best, reserving BsonDocument for strict BSON-typing needs.
- [3 Steps to Optimize Your Queries for Speed](https://dev.to/franckpachot/3-steps-to-optimize-your-queries-for-speed-59a2) - 2026-01-05 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3150942.json](devto/articles/3150942.json)
  Evidence note: An e-commerce order example walks from a normalized MongoDB model to an embedded model, then adds and refines an index, cutting a full collection scan down to reading only the needed documents.
- [UPDATE...RETURNING in MongoDB: ACID and idempotency with findOneAndUpdate()](https://dev.to/franckpachot/acid-and-idempotent-update-returning-in-mongodb-with-findoneandupdate-955) - 2026-01-04 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/3146807.json](devto/articles/3146807.json)
  Evidence note: Replacing updateOne-then-find with a single findOneAndUpdate() call, mirroring PostgreSQL's UPDATE...RETURNING, closes the window where a concurrent withdrawal made Bob's balance inconsistent.
- [FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED in MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-equivalent-to-for-update-skip-locked-1m15) - 2026-01-01 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, MySQL | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/3141770.json](devto/articles/3141770.json)
  Evidence note: Answering a Reddit question about multi-process document claiming, an optimistic-lock field written per worker lets MongoDB coordinate exclusive document processing without FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED.

### 2025

- [Why isn't "majority" the default read concern in MongoDB?](https://dev.to/franckpachot/why-isnt-majority-the-default-read-concern-in-mongodb-2782) - 2025-12-31 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/3135668.json](devto/articles/3135668.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB defaults to a lower-latency read concern optimized for asynchronous event-driven microservices, but switching to majority read concern gives the same durability and isolation SQL users expect.
- [MongoDB read and write concerns compared to PostgreSQL synchronous commit](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-readwrite-vs-postgresql-synchronous-replication-2ni8) - 2025-12-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Backup & Recovery; archive: [devto/articles/3130213.json](devto/articles/3130213.json)
  Evidence note: Mapping six myths from a PGConf.DE talk on synchronous replication, w:1 vs w:majority write concern and each read concern in MongoDB are matched against PostgreSQL's synchronous_commit behavior.
- [No Foreign Keys in MongoDB: Rethinking Referential Integrity](https://dev.to/franckpachot/no-foreign-keys-in-mongodb-rethinking-referential-integrity-1b9b) - 2025-12-25 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3125389.json](devto/articles/3125389.json)
  Evidence note: Instead of raising an immediate error like a SQL foreign key, MongoDB validates relationships at the application level, trading immediate serializable-read checks for asynchronous verification.
- [Adaptive Join in Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/adaptive-join-in-amazon-aurora-5ha0) - 2025-12-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Amazon Aurora | Query Optimization, Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3123378.json](devto/articles/3123378.json)
  Evidence note: Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL's adaptive join defers the nested-loop-versus-hash-join decision until execution, similar to MongoDB's multi-planner and Oracle's buffered-row deferred plan selection.
- [Unnesting Scalar Subqueries into Left Outer Joins in SQL in Aurora and PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/unnesting-scalar-subqueries-into-left-outer-joins-in-sql-556k) - 2025-12-23 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/3122674.json](devto/articles/3122674.json)
  Evidence note: Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL adds a planner transformation, absent in open-source PostgreSQL, that rewrites a correlated scalar subquery in the SELECT list into a LEFT OUTER JOIN for a better plan.
- [Financial Databases: SQL Is Not the Only Option – From ACID Theory to Banking Reality](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/financial-databases-sql-only-option-from-acid-theory-banking-pachot-vbtee) - 2025-12-22 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [linkedin/articles/vbtee.json](linkedin/articles/vbtee.json)
  Evidence note: Shows how ATM withdrawals, currency transfers, and card settlement span systems as short atomic operations coordinated through idempotency and reconciliation, rather than one long SQL transaction.
- [JSONB vs. BSON: Tracing PostgreSQL and MongoDB Wire Protocols](https://dev.to/franckpachot/jsonb-vs-bson-tracing-postgresql-and-mongodb-wire-protocols-1m51) - 2025-12-21 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3118738.json](devto/articles/3118738.json)
  Evidence note: JSONB is purely PostgreSQL's internal storage format requiring full detoasting to read one field, whereas BSON is MongoDB's native wire format used unchanged by drivers, network, memory, and disk.
- [End-of-Year Thoughts on PostgreSQL (2025)](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/end-of-year-thoughts-postgresql-2026-franck-pachot-wka4e) - 2025-12-20 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Events & Community; archive: [linkedin/articles/wka4e.json](linkedin/articles/wka4e.json)
  Evidence note: Reviews PostgreSQL's 2025 developments across version 18 I/O and planner work, search extensions, cloud storage architectures, sharding, acquisitions, and an increasingly fragmented compatible ecosystem.
- [Atlas Search scoring calculation and why Lucene scores differ from Elasticsearch and pg_(text)search](https://dev.to/franckpachot/atlas-search-score-details-the-bm25-calculation-2g55) - 2025-12-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/3116342.json](devto/articles/3116342.json)
  Evidence note: Comparing emoji-based test documents shows MongoDB Atlas Search's BM25 score is a constant factor of 2.2 lower than Elasticsearch's, though score details reveal the same ranking order.
- [Using $sql in Oracle Database instead of explain("executionStats")](https://dev.to/franckpachot/the-sql-aggregation-stage-in-oracle-database-to-replace-explainexecutionstats-3clj) - 2025-12-17 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3110780.json](devto/articles/3110780.json)
  Evidence note: On a one-million-document MongoDB-emulation collection indexed for Equality-Sort-Range, Oracle Database's $sql aggregation stage exposes the relational execution plan behind a millisecond query.
- [Many-to-One: Stronger Relationship Design with MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/many-to-one-in-mongodb-embed-or-reference-lookup-or-find-i58) - 2025-12-15 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3104255.json](devto/articles/3104255.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike a normalized SQL one-to-many schema built independent of access patterns, MongoDB's document model lets a one-to-many relationship be implemented multiple ways based on known cardinality.
- [No, MongoDB Does Not Mean Skipping Design](https://thenewstack.io/rethinking-data-integrity-why-domain-driven-design-is-crucial/) - 2025-12-12 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Migration & Compatibility, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3102686.json](devto/articles/3102686.json)
  Evidence note: Rejects the assumption that a document database forces careless design, arguing developers invest as much in MongoDB's domain model as DBAs do in normalized SQL schemas requiring migration scripts.
- [New Relic - CPU usage (%) and Load Average](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/new-relic-cpu-usage-and-load-average-3o4) - 2025-12-03 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/3027302.json](devto/articles/3027302.json)
  Evidence note: Demonstrated live at the New Relic AWS re:Invent booth, a `yes` CPU-bound loop followed by 32-thread synchronous fio disk writes shows how New Relic's CPU-percentage and load-average graphs diverge.
- [Script to list MongoDB collection URI (to map to WiredTiger files)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/script-to-list-mongodb-collection-uri-to-map-to-wiredtiger-files-4k2n) - 2025-11-29 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/3069329.json](devto/articles/3069329.json)
  Evidence note: Because strace -y shows only raw WiredTiger .wt filenames like collection-7e76acd8, a script maps those UUIDs back to human-readable MongoDB collection names for I/O tracing.
- [MongoDB Index Intersection (and PostgreSQL Bitmap-and)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-index-intersection-and-postgresql-bitmap-scan-40a6) - 2025-11-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3065018.json](devto/articles/3065018.json)
  Evidence note: Given two single-field indexes on 100,000 documents, MongoDB rarely picks index intersection the way PostgreSQL uses Bitmap Scan, suggesting a compound index is usually the better fix.
- [What writeConcern: {w: 1} Really Means: Isolation and Durability](https://dev.to/mongodb/what-writeconcern-w-1-really-means-isolation-and-durability-3glk) - 2025-11-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3035325.json](devto/articles/3035325.json)
  Evidence note: A w:1 MongoDB write is acknowledged by the primary before replication completes, risking rollback on primary failure; since MongoDB 5.0, most PSS clusters implicitly default to w:majority instead.
- [Data Locality vs. Independence (NoSQL vs. SQL)](https://thenewstack.io/why-store-together-access-together-matters-for-your-database) - 2025-11-23 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3051440.json](devto/articles/3051440.json)
  Evidence note: 'Store together what is accessed together' lets document databases fetch related data in one I/O call, while SQL's Codd-style data independence deliberately separates logical and physical layout.
- [INNER JOIN and LEFT OUTER JOIN in MongoDB (with $lookup and $unwind)](https://dev.to/mongodb/inner-join-and-left-outer-join-in-mongodb-with-lookup-and-unwind-2ge4) - 2025-11-21 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/3041533.json](devto/articles/3041533.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike a flat SQL JOIN that repeats one-side values for each many-side row, $lookup nests matched documents into an array per parent, so $unwind is needed to reproduce the flattened SQL output.
- [Nested Loop and Hash Join for MongoDB $lookup](https://dev.to/mongodb/nested-loop-and-hash-join-for-mongodb-lookup-259d) - 2025-11-17 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/3032189.json](devto/articles/3032189.json)
  Evidence note: Answering a Reddit optimization question about a users-to-profiles $lookup with a status filter and $limit, MongoDB's planner picks between nested-loop and hash-style join algorithms much like SQL.
- [Amazon DocumentDB New Query Planner (version 2)](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/amazon-documentdb-new-query-planner-2dh0) - 2025-11-14 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/3016195.json](devto/articles/3016195.json)
  Evidence note: On a 20,000-order collection indexed by country, product, and creation date, Amazon DocumentDB's new query planner (NQP, version 2.0) markedly speeds up a last-ten-orders-per-country query.
- [How does it scale? A basic OLTP benchmark on MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/how-does-it-scale-the-most-basic-benchmark-on-mongodb-p9b) - 2025-11-11 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/3012967.json](devto/articles/3012967.json)
  Evidence note: Batches of 1,000 inserted operations per account, queried by category and most-recent date through a compound index, keep response times stable as the accounts collection grows, avoiding full scans.
- [Covering Index for $group/$sum in MongoDB Aggregation (With Hint)](https://dev.to/mongodb/covering-index-for-group-in-mongodb-aggregation-with-hint-4e6m) - 2025-11-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2982347.json](devto/articles/2982347.json)
  Evidence note: On 50,000 documents with 1,000 distinct group values, a $group/$sum aggregation only gets a covered, document-fetch-free plan when the index is explicitly forced with hint(), unlike DISTINCT_SCAN.
- [Advanced Query Capabilities 👉🏻 aggregation pipelines](https://dev.to/franckpachot/advanced-query-capabilities-aggregation-pipelines-137m) - 2025-10-22 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/2907359.json](devto/articles/2907359.json)
  Evidence note: Rebuts a migration-tool vendor's claim that only SQL provides strong consistency and rich querying, citing MongoDB's ACID multi-document transactions and aggregation-pipeline power.
- [First/Last per Group: PostgreSQL DISTINCT ON and MongoDB DISTINCT_SCAN Performance](https://dev.to/mongodb/firstlast-per-group-postgresql-distinct-on-and-mongodb-distinctscan-performance-1e9d) - 2025-10-03 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2889160.json](devto/articles/2889160.json)
  Evidence note: Answering Stack Overflow's most-frequent PostgreSQL question, first-per-group rows, DISTINCT ON is compared against MongoDB's $sort plus $first/$last, triggering an efficient DISTINCT_SCAN plan.
- [WiredTigerHS.wt: MongoDB MVCC Durable History Store](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-mvcc-durable-history-store-wiredtigerhswt-mn2) - 2025-09-28 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Security; archive: [devto/articles/2850562.json](devto/articles/2850562.json)
  Evidence note: Following a No-Force/No-Steal policy, MongoDB's WiredTiger keeps uncommitted changes only in memory and stores older MVCC versions separately in the WiredTigerHS.wt history-store file.
- [MongoDB Search Index Internals With Luke (Lucene Toolbox GUI Tool)](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-search-index-internals-with-luke-lucene-toolbox-gui-tool-2842) - 2025-09-21 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2859516.json](devto/articles/2859516.json)
  Evidence note: The Lucene Toolbox GUI tool Luke inspects a MongoDB Atlas Local search index built on the same fruit-emoji dataset, revealing what a dynamic-field search index actually stores under WiredTiger.
- [Text Search With MongoDB (BM25 TF-IDF) and PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/mongodb/text-search-with-mongodb-and-postgresql-full-text-search-1blg) - 2025-09-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, AI & Search, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/2854549.json](devto/articles/2854549.json)
  Evidence note: Nine short fruit-emoji documents of varying length and term frequency compare MongoDB's Atlas Search BM25 default ranking against PostgreSQL's built-in full-text search TF-IDF-based scoring.
- [Combine Two JSON Collections with Nested Arrays: MongoDB and PostgreSQL Aggregations](https://dev.to/franckpachot/combine-two-json-collections-with-nested-arrays-mongodb-and-postgresql-aggregations-30k2) - 2025-09-18 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2847233.json](devto/articles/2847233.json)
  Evidence note: Solving a Stack Overflow request to merge two JSONB tables of daily cash-register sales into one grouped, nested-array total, the same aggregation logic is implemented in PostgreSQL and MongoDB.
- [MongoDB Multikey Indexes and Index Bound Optimization](https://dev.to/franckpachot/mongodb-multikey-indexes-and-index-bound-optimization-ol9) - 2025-09-16 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/2845065.json](devto/articles/2845065.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB tracks whether an indexed field ever holds an array, letting it skip generating one index entry per array-element combination and instead optimize scalar-only compound-index range bounds.
- [MongoDB Internals: How Collections and Indexes Are Stored in WiredTiger](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-internals-how-collections-and-indexes-are-stored-in-wiredtiger-2ed) - 2025-09-14 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2841989.json](devto/articles/2841989.json)
  Evidence note: Using the wt command-line tool inside a Docker container, this traces the _mdb_catalog metadata, B-tree page layout, BSON storage, and multi-key array handling behind MongoDB's WiredTiger files.
- [Resilience of MongoDB's WiredTiger Storage Engine to Disk Failure Compared to PostgreSQL and Oracle](https://dev.to/mongodb/resilience-of-mongodbs-wiredtiger-storage-engine-to-disk-failure-compared-to-postgresql-and-oracle-h9f) - 2025-09-08 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2829275.json](devto/articles/2829275.json)
  Evidence note: Deliberately corrupting storage at the block level in lab containers compares how PostgreSQL 18's default checksums, Oracle, and MongoDB's WiredTiger each detect, or fail to detect, a lost write.
- [DocumentDB: Comparing Emulation Internals with MongoDB](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/documentdb-comparing-emulations-with-mongodb-4cec) - 2025-09-01 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Amazon Aurora, DocumentDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2809970.json](devto/articles/2809970.json)
  Evidence note: Now under the Linux Foundation with AWS joining, Microsoft's DocumentDB extension emulates MongoDB storage, indexing, and querying on PostgreSQL, though it can't replace native document storage.
- [Updates to the Same Value: MongoDB Optimization](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-optimizes-updates-to-the-same-value-1f2k) - 2025-08-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/2804512.json](devto/articles/2804512.json)
  Evidence note: MongoDB skips rewriting a document and its index entries when an update sets a field to the value it already holds, unlike a PostgreSQL SET val=val update, which still generates WAL records.
- [PostgreSQL JSONB Size Limits to Prevent TOAST Slicing](https://dev.to/franckpachot/postgresql-jsonb-size-limits-to-prevent-toast-slicing-9e8) - 2025-08-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/2803581.json](devto/articles/2803581.json)
  Evidence note: TOAST activates once a row exceeds roughly 2KB, a quarter of the default 8KB page, first compressing or relocating large JSONB values before slicing them into chunked TOAST-table rows.
- [Embedding Into JSONB Still Feels Like a JOIN for Large Documents](https://dev.to/mongodb/embedding-into-jsonb-still-feels-like-a-join-for-large-documents-3nd0) - 2025-08-24 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/2787817.json](devto/articles/2787817.json)
  Evidence note: Unless a JSONB document fits within a fraction of PostgreSQL's 8KB page, reading embedded data still triggers a per-document index lookup through TOAST, behaving like the nested-loop join it avoids.
- [MongoDB Arrays: Sort Order and Comparison](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-arrays-sort-order-and-comparison-d9d) - 2025-08-18 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2777174.json](devto/articles/2777174.json)
  Evidence note: Because a MongoDB field can be a scalar in one document and an array in another, sort and comparison semantics must handle both cases consistently, unlike SQL's fixed single-value column typing.
- [Why Doesn't Oracle Multi-Value Index Optimize .sort() Like MongoDB Does With its Multi-Key Index?](https://dev.to/mongodb/why-doesnt-oracle-multi-value-index-optimize-sort-like-mongodb-does-with-its-multi-key-index-2b5c) - 2025-08-15 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2775490.json](devto/articles/2775490.json)
  Evidence note: Inspecting Oracle 23ai's new Multi-Value Index (MVI) internals on a JSON array column shows it lacks the ordering support that lets MongoDB's multi-key index optimize a sorted query.
- [Does PostgreSQL support as much "schema flexibility" as MongoDB? Not for indexing!](https://dev.to/mongodb/does-postgresql-support-as-much-schema-flexibility-as-mongodb-not-for-indexing-412g) - 2025-08-12 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2770491.json](devto/articles/2770491.json)
  Evidence note: Every documented order-array example from a Medium claim that PostgreSQL matches MongoDB's schema flexibility either returns wrong results or fails to use an index when reproduced.
- [PostgreSQL UUID: Bulk insert with UUIDv7 vs UUIDv4](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/postgresql-uuid-bulk-insert-with-uuidv7-vs-uuidv4-4oca) - 2025-08-11 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2768109.json](devto/articles/2768109.json)
  Evidence note: Bulk-loading ten million rows shows PostgreSQL 18's time-ordered uuidv7() improves cache efficiency over random uuidv4() during bulk ingest, even though both share the same uuid datatype and column.
- [Joining and grouping on array fields in MongoDB may require using $unwind before applying $group or $lookup](https://dev.to/mongodb/joining-and-grouping-on-array-fields-in-mongodb-may-require-using-unwind-before-applying-group-or-41nj) - 2025-08-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2728789.json](devto/articles/2728789.json)
  Evidence note: Grouping or joining on a nested team-member array in MongoDB needs an explicit $unwind before $group or $lookup, since flattened tabular results aren't automatic like in normalized SQL.
- [MongoDB indexing internals: .showRecordId() and .hint({$natural:1})](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-indexing-internals-showrecordid-and-hintnatural1-4cpl) - 2025-08-07 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB, MySQL | Indexing, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/2748599.json](devto/articles/2748599.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike Oracle's ROWID or PostgreSQL's CTID heap pointers, MongoDB's WiredTiger clusters documents in a B+Tree keyed by an internal RecordId, visible via .showRecordId() and $natural hints.
- [Transaction performance 👉🏻 retry with backoff](https://dev.to/franckpachot/transaction-performance-retry-with-backoff-12lm) - 2025-08-05 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/2752959.json](devto/articles/2752959.json)
  Evidence note: Re-analyzing a 2019 EDB benchmark that fueled the 'MongoDB transactions are slow' myth shows its code never implemented retry-with-backoff for fail-on-conflict concurrency.
- [Why MongoDB skips indexes when flattening or renaming sub-document fields in $project before $match aggregation pipeline](https://dev.to/mongodb/why-mongodb-skips-indexes-when-flattening-or-renaming-sub-document-fields-in-project-before-match-1o6d) - 2025-08-04 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2750693.json](devto/articles/2750693.json)
  Evidence note: Renaming a dotted sub-document path like contact.email in a $project stage before a $match can alter its semantics enough that MongoDB's aggregation optimizer stops using the underlying index.
- [MongoDB High Availability: Replica Set in a Docker Lab](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-high-availability-replicaset-in-a-docker-lab-4jlc) - 2025-08-02 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/2669399.json](devto/articles/2669399.json)
  Evidence note: A three-node Docker Compose replica set, with tc and strace injecting artificial network and disk latency, demonstrates how write concern choices trade latency against durability during replication.
- [Lock-Free Wait-on-Conflict and Fail-on-Conflict in MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/lock-free-wait-on-conflict-and-fail-on-conflict-in-mongodb-372h) - 2025-07-23 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2716028.json](devto/articles/2716028.json)
  Evidence note: Redefines pessimistic and optimistic concurrency as Wait-on-Conflict, blocking until a conflict resolves, versus Fail-on-Conflict, MongoDB's approach of erroring immediately so the app retries.
- [.hint() in MongoDB is different that SQL optimizer hints: they force indexes](https://dev.to/mongodb/hint-in-mongodb-is-different-that-sql-optimizer-hints-they-force-indexes-4c33) - 2025-07-22 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Migration & Compatibility, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2714273.json](devto/articles/2714273.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike most SQL optimizer hints that merely suggest a plan, MongoDB's .hint() forcibly overrides the query planner's index choice, working like a NoSQL-style direct index reference in a query.
- [Database latency with PostgreSQL and MongoDB: it's the data model that makes it fast](https://dev.to/mongodb/client-server-latency-in-postgresql-and-mongodb-81h) - 2025-07-21 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2706969.json](devto/articles/2706969.json)
  Evidence note: Comparing a pgbench multi-statement PostgreSQL transaction, a single auto-commit call, and MongoDB's single- and multi-document transactions shows embedding data in one document cuts round trips.
- [$isArray: [💬,💬,💬] ❌ - Arrays are Argument Lists in MongoDB Aggregation Pipeline](https://dev.to/mongodb/isarray-arrays-are-argument-lists-in-mongodb-aggregation-pipeline-1cch) - 2025-07-20 | Dev.to | MongoDB | General Database; archive: [devto/articles/2707842.json](devto/articles/2707842.json)
  Evidence note: Testing $isArray:[42,42,42] throws an argument-count error instead of evaluating an array, since MongoDB treats a bracketed list as multiple arguments unless wrapped with $literal.
- [Sequences in MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/sequences-in-mongodb-with-gap-and-no-gap-transactional-57e4) - 2025-07-18 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | General Database; archive: [devto/articles/2703992.json](devto/articles/2703992.json)
  Evidence note: Instead of a dedicated sequence object, MongoDB generates gap-free identifiers with a single atomic findOneAndUpdate() using $inc on a sequence document, unlike SQL sequences which can leave gaps.
- [Data Modeling: Is Normalization Still Relevant?](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/data-modeling-normalization-still-relevant-franck-pachot-rp1pe) - 2025-07-18 | LinkedIn | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [linkedin/articles/rp1pe.json](linkedin/articles/rp1pe.json)
  Evidence note: Revisits Codd's original normalization objectives and evaluates how schema evolution, consistency, querying, and workload independence change when applications use domain-driven document aggregates.
- [Wildcard Indexes: MongoDB flexibility joins query performance](https://dev.to/franckpachot/wildcard-indexes--19la) - 2025-07-10 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2672857.json](devto/articles/2672857.json)
  Evidence note: Indexed against a one-million-video YouTube statistics dataset, a MongoDB wildcard index covers every attribute of an embedded accessControl sub-object, working like an UNPIVOT over index entries.
- [ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN](https://dev.to/mongodb/alter-table-add-column-2nlp) - 2025-07-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2665934.json](devto/articles/2665934.json)
  Evidence note: While MongoDB's flexible per-document schema needs no DDL for new fields, modern PostgreSQL's ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN with a constant default no longer rewrites the whole table or holds a long lock.
- [Erik (author of Lucene in Action) is starting a series on search indexes](https://dev.to/franckpachot/erik-is-starting-a-series-on-search-indexes-5ba8) - 2025-07-03 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/2651464.json](devto/articles/2651464.json)
  Evidence note: Points to Erik Hatcher, author of Lucene in Action, launching his own dev.to series exploring search indexes, cross-linked for readers following MongoDB's Lucene-based Atlas Search coverage.
- [PostgreSQL Extensions and the "Anarchy in the Database" VLDB paper](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/postgresql-extensions-anarchy-database-vldb-paper-franck-pachot-mjo4e) - 2025-07-01 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | Internals & Storage, Security, Operations & Observability, Events & Community; archive: [linkedin/articles/mjo4e.json](linkedin/articles/mjo4e.json)
  Evidence note: Compares PostgreSQL extensions and forks, arguing both can alter core behavior and carry compatibility risk while differing mainly in packaging, deployment, and how changes are maintained.
- [Strong consistency 👉🏻 MongoDB highly available durable writes (Replication)](https://dev.to/mongodb/strong-consistency-mongodb-highly-available-durable-writes-2j2k) - 2025-06-30 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/2638775.json](devto/articles/2638775.json)
  Evidence note: Extending an earlier strace-traced durability test to a three-container Docker Compose replica set shows how MongoDB layers Raft-inspired leader election atop already-durable writes.
- [Flush to disk on commit 👉🏻 MongoDB durable writes (ACID)](https://dev.to/mongodb/committed-writes-are-flushed-to-disk-mongodb-durability-12o9) - 2025-06-28 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2632801.json](devto/articles/2632801.json)
  Evidence note: Responding to a viral Twitter thread's FUD, strace and GDB trace a local MongoDB server proving its write-ahead journal is flushed to disk before an acknowledged write is actually committed.
- [No pre-filtering in pgvector means reduced ANN recall (impact on Vector Search accuracy)](https://dev.to/mongodb/no-pre-filtering-in-pgvector-means-reduced-ann-recall-1aa1) - 2025-06-24 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/2615691.json](devto/articles/2615691.json)
  Evidence note: A demo filtering vector similarity search by tenant_id shows PostgreSQL's pgvector applies the WHERE predicate after the nearest-neighbor search, reducing recall versus genuine pre-filtering.
- [faster $lookup after $group in MongoDB aggregation pipeline (Performance Optimization Tips)](https://dev.to/mongodb/faster-lookup-after-group-in-mongodb-aggregation-pipeline-5fbp) - 2025-06-22 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2610586.json](devto/articles/2610586.json)
  Evidence note: Against a thousand-document dimension collection and a million-document fact collection, running $group before $lookup resolves far fewer references than looking up first per document.
- [One million $lookup challenge (performance comparison)](https://dev.to/mongodb/one-million-lookup-challenge-mongodb-slow-join-1kao) - 2025-06-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2603901.json](devto/articles/2603901.json)
  Evidence note: Confirms $lookup isn't built to join scalar values across a million documents; embedding avoids the join entirely, since SQL normalization mainly protects interactive-user data integrity.
- [$lookup: more than just a SQL join (understand performance)](https://dev.to/mongodb/lookup-is-more-than-just-a-sql-join-3b90) - 2025-06-17 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2598230.json](devto/articles/2598230.json)
  Evidence note: Although $lookup resembles a SQL LEFT JOIN, it intersects two sets like PostgreSQL's array && operator and returns matches nested as an array instead of duplicating outer rows per match.
- [Queries on JSON 👉🏻 compound indexes (Equality, Sort, Range) in MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/queries-on-json-compound-indexes-equality-sort-range-2lce) - 2025-06-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2594489.json](devto/articles/2594489.json)
  Evidence note: Rebuilding the four GitHub-archive queries from an EDB benchmark claiming Postgres beats MongoDB on JSON workloads shows poor MongoDB index design, not the database, explains the slow results.
- [DuckDB to query MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/duckdb-to-query-mongodb-3lnk) - 2025-06-13 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2588181.json](devto/articles/2588181.json)
  Evidence note: Combining pg_duckdb and mongo_fdw PostgreSQL extensions lets single-process, analytical DuckDB query MongoDB collections through foreign tables, since DuckDB has no native MongoDB connector.
- [Comparison of JOINS 👉🏻 aggregation pipeline and CTEs](https://dev.to/mongodb/comparison-of-joins-mongodb-vs-postgresql-aggregation-pipeline-4h12) - 2025-06-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2576478.json](devto/articles/2576478.json)
  Evidence note: Critiques an EDB blog's employee-department $lookup-then-$group pipeline claiming 'joins are brittle in MongoDB,' showing the example itself violates recommended aggregation design practice.
- ["Schema Later" considered harmful 👉🏻 schema validation (enforced data consistency)](https://dev.to/mongodb/schema-later-considered-harmful-use-schema-validation-in-mongodb-364o) - 2025-06-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2576242.json](devto/articles/2576242.json)
  Evidence note: Responds to an EDB write-up inserting junk data into an unvalidated MongoDB collection, showing MongoDB's schema validation enforces the same schema-on-write guarantees the SQL example used.
- [Isolation Level for MongoDB Multi-Document Transactions (Strong Consistency)](https://dev.to/mongodb/isolation-level-for-mongodb-multi-document-transactions-3lfi) - 2025-06-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2569051.json](devto/articles/2569051.json)
  Evidence note: Porting Martin Kleppmann's Hermitage isolation tests from PostgreSQL to MongoDB shows its snapshot read concern matches the multi-document transaction isolation of other MVCC SQL databases.
- [JSONB DeTOASTing (read amplification) when using PostgreSQL as a Document Database](https://dev.to/mongodb/jsonb-detoasting-read-amplification-4ikj) - 2025-06-04 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2550082.json](devto/articles/2550082.json)
  Evidence note: Storing 100,000 user profiles with a large bio field in one JSONB column triggers PostgreSQL's TOAST compression and splitting, causing read amplification absent in native document storage.
- [No HOT updates on JSONB (write amplification) performance impact](https://dev.to/mongodb/no-hot-updates-on-jsonb-13k7) - 2025-05-31 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2549165.json](devto/articles/2549165.json)
  Evidence note: Because PostgreSQL indexes reference rows by physical ctid, updating even one field inside a large JSONB document forces new index entries everywhere, blocking the cheaper Heap-Only-Tuple update path.
- [$graphLookup (Connect By / Recursive Query)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/graphlookup-connect-by-recursive-query-e7j) - 2025-05-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2525374.json](devto/articles/2525374.json)
  Evidence note: On a YouTube-videos collection where each document lists an array of relatedVideos IDs, $graphLookup recursively traverses that embedded graph without needing extra indexes beyond the original schema.
- [$elemMatch and Multi-Key Indexes](https://dev.to/franckpachot/elemmatch-and-multi-key-indexes-i6e) - 2025-05-28 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2522931.json](devto/articles/2522931.json)
  Evidence note: A multi-key index on views.daily.data optimizes a single-array-bound filter like views over ten million, but two separate bounds without $elemMatch match mismatched values across array elements.
- [Sort on Array with Multi-Key Index](https://dev.to/franckpachot/sort-on-array-with-multi-key-index-2nhn) - 2025-05-27 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2522746.json](devto/articles/2522746.json)
  Evidence note: A descending multi-key index on views.daily.data lets MongoDB sort videos by highest daily view count directly from the index, using per-array-element entries instead of scanning documents.
- [Google Firestore with MongoDB compatibility - index limitations](https://dev.to/franckpachot/firestore-with-mongodb-compatibility-testing-pagination-queries-jmf) - 2025-05-27 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2532950.json](devto/articles/2532950.json)
  Evidence note: Testing the same country-and-product order-history query against Google Firestore's MongoDB-compatible API confirms it, like other emulations, is syntax-compatible but not performance-compatible.
- [Equality with Multiple Values, Preserving Sort for Pagination](https://dev.to/franckpachot/equality-with-multiple-values-preserving-sort-for-pagination-ddh) - 2025-05-26 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2516063.json](devto/articles/2516063.json)
  Evidence note: A category/publishedDate/duration compound index still serves a query listing videos across several categories, since MongoDB merges up to 200 per-value index scans while keeping sort order.
- [No Index for LIKE on JSONB with Array in the Path (GIN limitation)](https://dev.to/mongodb/no-index-for-like-on-jsonb-with-array-in-the-path-hbe) - 2025-05-24 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2522167.json](devto/articles/2522167.json)
  Evidence note: Storing multiple email addresses as a JSONB array shows PostgreSQL's GIN index, already needed once an array enters the path, still cannot accelerate a LIKE pattern search on those array elements.
- [No Index Only Scan on JSONB Fields (even on scalar)](https://dev.to/mongodb/no-index-only-scan-on-jsonb-fields-and-with-even-scalar-6n6) - 2025-05-23 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2517428.json](devto/articles/2517428.json)
  Evidence note: Answering a Reddit thread about a slow GROUP BY on 100 million JSONB rows, an expression B-Tree index supports scalar equality, but GIN cannot enable an Index Only Scan for GROUP BY or ORDER BY.
- [Intro to PostgreSQL with JSONB compared to MongoDB, a general purpose document database](https://dev.to/mongodb/postgresql-with-jsonb-and-mongodb-with-schema-2nh0) - 2025-05-23 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2517414.json](devto/articles/2517414.json)
  Evidence note: Opens a series contrasting PostgreSQL's block format, planner, and index limits against MongoDB's native storage, arguing JSONB only superficially resembles a document database.
- [B-Tree for Equality, Sort, Range (indexing strategies in MongoDB)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/b-tree-for-equality-sort-range-2ego) - 2025-05-23 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2515074.json](devto/articles/2515074.json)
  Evidence note: Frames MongoDB index design around four parts: an equality prefix for partitioning, a sort field for range and pagination, extra range-filter fields, and covering fields to skip document fetches.
- [Search Index for Reporting](https://dev.to/franckpachot/search-index-for-reporting-1d7n) - 2025-05-22 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2515069.json](devto/articles/2515069.json)
  Evidence note: A MongoDB Atlas Search index over the YouTube statistics dataset, with dynamic:false and typed fields like token and number, serves near-real-time OLAP reporting isolated from the OLTP workload.
- [Sampling Without Index](https://dev.to/franckpachot/sampling-without-index-b0d) - 2025-05-22 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2515061.json](devto/articles/2515061.json)
  Evidence note: Before any indexes exist on the imported YouTube dataset, a $sample stage still runs a millisecond category breakdown, but only over a random subset rather than the full collection.
- [Indexing for New Use Cases Within the MongoDB Document Model (tutorial)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/indexing-for-new-use-cases-within-the-mongodb-document-model-tutorial-2hdm) - 2025-05-22 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2510263.json](devto/articles/2510263.json)
  Evidence note: Imports a Kaggle YouTube video-statistics dataset shaped for OLAP reporting, then adds a small set of secondary indexes to serve diverse new OLTP queries without altering the document schema.
- [Using a Star Query on MongoDB Atlas Search Index](https://dev.to/mongodb/htap-using-a-star-query-on-mongodb-atlas-search-index-17p5) - 2025-05-06 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2461605.json](devto/articles/2461605.json)
  Evidence note: A healthcare example shows MongoDB's Lucene-based Atlas Search index running star-transformation-style ad-hoc analytics on the operational schema, without a separate star-schema database.
- [Amazon DocumentDB and multi-key indexing](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/amazon-documentdb-and-multi-key-indexing-147d) - 2025-05-05 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Amazon Aurora, DocumentDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2453549.json](devto/articles/2453549.json)
  Evidence note: Testing MongoDB 5.0-compatible Amazon DocumentDB with the same YouTube-statistics collection shows it, like every other emulation tested, can't efficiently run find().sort().limit() on arrays.
- [Amazon DocumentDB != Microsoft DocumentDB extension for PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/amazon-documentdb-microsoft-documentdb-extension-for-postgresql-210f) - 2025-05-04 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Amazon Aurora, DocumentDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2458709.json](devto/articles/2458709.json)
  Evidence note: Clarifies that Amazon DocumentDB, a MongoDB-compatible service resembling Aurora internally, is unrelated to Microsoft's separately named open-source DocumentDB extension used in vCore Cosmos DB.
- [RUM instead of GIN but same limitations on JSON paths (tested on FerretDB 17-0.107.0)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/microsoft-documentdb-rum-instead-of-gin-but-same-limitations-on-json-paths-48kn) - 2025-05-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2451522.json](devto/articles/2451522.json)
  Evidence note: Testing FerretDB v2's PostgreSQL-based DocumentDB extension shows RUM indexes, which replaced GIN for BSON storage, still share GIN's inability to optimize range or pagination queries on JSON paths.
- [Querying embedded arrays in JSON (PostgreSQL JSONB and MongoDB documents)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/query-and-index-json-array-fields-in-postgresql-and-mongodb-3md) - 2025-04-30 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2449915.json](devto/articles/2449915.json)
  Evidence note: Embedding book reviews as a nested JSONB array avoids a separate table, but querying that one-to-many array in PostgreSQL needs a GIN index and JSON path operators, not a simple expression index.
- [DB-Engines Ranking Method Reviewed](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/db-engines-ranking-method-reviewed-franck-pachot-wgyie) - 2025-04-29 | LinkedIn | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [linkedin/articles/wgyie.json](linkedin/articles/wgyie.json)
  Evidence note: Examines DB-Engines ranking inputs and historical anomalies, arguing search mentions, forum questions, job listings, profiles, and social buzz do not reliably measure database adoption or suitability.
- [PostgreSQL aborts the transactions on error](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/postgresql-rollback-on-error-515a) - 2025-04-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2440910.json](devto/articles/2440910.json)
  Evidence note: After a duplicate-key INSERT fails inside an open transaction, PostgreSQL's prompt shows a failed '!' state where even issuing COMMIT silently executes an implicit ROLLBACK instead.
- [Index Only Scan on JSON Documents in MongoDB, with covering and multi-key indexes](https://dev.to/mongodb/index-only-scan-on-json-documents-with-covering-and-multi-key-indexes-in-mongodb-53h4) - 2025-04-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2436911.json](devto/articles/2436911.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike PostgreSQL's GIN indexes on JSONB arrays, which can't support range filters, sorting, or projection coverage, MongoDB's multi-key indexes natively enable a covering Index Only Scan.
- [A benchmark is published by a database vendor and guess what? 🥁 their database is faster 👏🏼](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/benchmark-published-database-vendor-guess-what-faster-franck-pachot-gt4le) - 2025-04-21 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [linkedin/articles/gt4le.json](linkedin/articles/gt4le.json)
  Evidence note: Reviews a vendor-published real-time analytics benchmark, identifying workload, configuration, and product-scope choices that make comparisons between purpose-built, embedded, and general-purpose databases misleading.
- [PostgreSQL JSONB Indexing Limitations with B-Tree and GIN](https://dev.to/mongodb/postgresql-jsonb-indexing-limitations-with-b-tree-and-gin-3851) - 2025-04-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2409682.json](devto/articles/2409682.json)
  Evidence note: Embedding order details as a JSONB array on a million-row orders table shows neither a B-Tree nor a GIN index can optimize selective predicates on both the order and the embedded line items together.
- [MongoDB With Search Indexes Queried as Kimball's Star Schema With Facts and Dimensions](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-with-search-indexes-queried-as-kimballs-star-schema-with-facts-and-dimensions-k1) - 2025-04-13 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Indexing, High Availability & Replication, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2404029.json](devto/articles/2404029.json)
  Evidence note: Exporting a relational fact-and-dimension dataset into MongoDB and adding search indexes shows the same document database can serve Kimball-style star-schema analytics without a warehouse.
- [Oracle Multi-Value Index and ORDER BY Pagination queries](https://dev.to/franckpachot/oracle-multi-value-index-and-order-by-pagination-queries-529e) - 2025-04-11 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB | Indexing, Migration & Compatibility, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/2397997.json](devto/articles/2397997.json)
  Evidence note: Using Oracle 23c's MongoDB-compatible API for the same nested order-details example, Oracle's multi-value JSON index still can't retrieve only the necessary entries the way MongoDB's index does.
- [Where SQL joins struggle but MongoDB documents shine](https://dev.to/mongodb/a-case-where-sql-joins-struggle-but-mongodb-documents-shine-11kj) - 2025-04-06 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2384679.json](devto/articles/2384679.json)
  Evidence note: Counters the claim that 'joins are slow' by showing where SQL join plans genuinely degrade, when selective predicates land on different tables after the join, a case MongoDB embedding avoids.
- [Foreign Keys: A must in SQL, but not in a Document Database?](https://dev.to/mongodb/foreign-keys-a-must-in-sql-but-not-in-a-document-database-2alf) - 2025-04-03 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2375803.json](devto/articles/2375803.json)
  Evidence note: SQL foreign keys exist to enforce first-normal-form table separation and give the query planner join-cardinality information, needs a document database's embedding largely removes by design.
- [❝ The World Is Too Messy for SQL to Work ❞](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/world-too-messy-sql-work-franck-pachot-jvq6e) - 2025-03-31 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [linkedin/articles/jvq6e.json](linkedin/articles/jvq6e.json)
  Evidence note: Responds to claims that SQL cannot handle messy data by showing how relational schemas can represent complex entities, multiple perspectives, and evolving business relationships without imposing one hierarchy.
- [MongoDB Vector Search Index, With Local Atlas and Ollama](https://dev.to/mongodb/vector-search-index-in-mongodb-with-local-atlas-and-ollama-1p8j) - 2025-03-26 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, AI & Search, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/2358692.json](devto/articles/2358692.json)
  Evidence note: Loading Eurovision song lyrics, in original language and English translation, into a local Atlas deployment builds and queries a MongoDB Vector Search index generated via Ollama embeddings.
- [MongoDB TTL and Disk Storage](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-ttl-and-disk-storage-2d35) - 2025-03-24 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2353684.json](devto/articles/2353684.json)
  Evidence note: A background delete-inspecting loop watches a TTL-indexed collection as documents are continuously inserted, revealing how MongoDB's TTL deletion manages disk storage and fragmentation.
- [MongoDB equivalent for PostgreSQL JSONB operations](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-equivalent-for-postgresql-jsonb-operations-hfo) - 2025-03-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2337250.json](devto/articles/2337250.json)
  Evidence note: Working through PostgreSQL's built-in GIN operator classes on a million-row JSONB table, each operation is matched to its MongoDB equivalent query on an identically structured document collection.
- [Normalization and Relational Division in SQL and MongoDB](https://dev.to/mongodb/normalization-and-relational-division-in-sql-and-mongodb-212l) - 2025-03-17 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2306481.json](devto/articles/2306481.json)
  Evidence note: Traces Codd's 1971 rationale for normalization, making tabular data accessible to non-programmer casual users at terminals, contrasting it with modern program-driven access to nested MongoDB data.
- [MongoDB Multi-Planner Optimizer and Plan Cache](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-multi-planner-optimizer-and-plan-cache-3g27) - 2025-03-13 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents, AI & Search; archive: [devto/articles/2331326.json](devto/articles/2331326.json)
  Evidence note: Contrasts rule-based and cost-based SQL optimizers with MongoDB's document model, which sidesteps join-ordering decisions by embedding, and its trial-based multi-planner caching the fastest index.
- [Relational and Document Data Modeling (50 years ago, 25 years ago, and 2025)](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/relational-document-data-modeling-50-years-ago-25-today-franck-pachot-c3hne) - 2025-03-11 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | Indexing, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, Development & Drivers; archive: [linkedin/articles/c3hne.json](linkedin/articles/c3hne.json)
  Evidence note: Traces relational and document modeling across changing application architectures, favoring normalized shared schemas for central repositories and aggregate-oriented documents for domain-owned services.
