- [Comparing Execution Plans: MongoDB vs. Compatible APIs](https://dev.to/mongodb/comparing-execution-plans-mongodb-vs-compatible-apis-46p6) - 2025-03-09 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, MongoDB, DocumentDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2321065.json](devto/articles/2321065.json)
  Evidence note: Testing an order/order-detail pagination query against Oracle's MongoDB-API-compatible autonomous database and native MongoDB shows MongoDB reading fewer rows and offering more indexing options.
- [Skip Scan in MongoDB with Range, Equality (RE) index as an alternative to Equality, Sort, Range (ESR)](https://dev.to/mongodb/skip-scan-in-mongodb-with-range-equality-re-index-as-an-alternative-to-equality-sort-range-176p) - 2025-02-27 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/2301189.json](devto/articles/2301189.json)
  Evidence note: Adding one document that skips the sort field shows an index ordered Equality-Range-Sort, rather than the usual Equality-Sort-Range, still lets MongoDB skip-scan multiple ranges efficiently.
- [Order-preserving or-expansion in MongoDB and $in:[,,] treated as a range skip scan, or exploded to an equality for the ESR rule](https://dev.to/mongodb/or-expansion-in-mongodb-and-how-in-is-treated-as-a-range-with-skip-scan-or-an-equality-for-2b38) - 2025-02-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MongoDB | Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/2301767.json](devto/articles/2301767.json)
  Evidence note: Querying an e:42 equality with an $in-based range on r and a sort on s shows MongoDB executes $in either as a multi-value equality skip scan or as several range scans, depending on field order.
- [MongoDB Equality, Sort, Range (ESR) without Equality (SR): add an unbounded range predicate on the indexed sort field](https://dev.to/mongodb/mongodb-equality-sort-range-esr-without-equality-sr-add-an-unbounded-range-predicate-on-the-2j9n) - 2025-02-11 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/2270247.json](devto/articles/2270247.json)
  Evidence note: Adding an unbounded {$gt:MinKey} equality predicate on the leading index field lets MongoDB choose a compound index for Sort-Range-only queries, tracked as MongoDB ticket SERVER-13197.
- [Aurora DSQL is different than Aurora, but Aurora DSQL belongs to Aurora (which belongs to RDS)](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-dsql-is-different-than-aurora-but-aurora-dsql-belongs-to-aurora-25e8) - 2025-02-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, MySQL, Amazon Aurora, Amazon DynamoDB | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2266720.json](devto/articles/2266720.json)
  Evidence note: Clarifies naming confusion: plain 'Aurora' is a single-instance PostgreSQL-compatible RDS database, while Aurora DSQL is a separately architected distributed database under the same Aurora brand.
- [Document Expiration with TTL Indexes in MongoDB](https://dev.to/franckpachot/document-expiration-with-ttl-indexes-in-mongodb-2007) - 2025-02-08 | Dev.to | MongoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2199299.json](devto/articles/2199299.json)
  Evidence note: Compares a manual delete job against MongoDB's expireAfterSeconds TTL index, showing metrics and internals differ, and queries after TTL deletion still skip removed pages efficiently.
- [My Journey to NoSQL and Document Databases](https://dev.to/franckpachot/my-journey-to-nosql-and-document-databases-4hdg) - 2025-02-06 | Dev.to | MongoDB | General Database; archive: [devto/articles/2263271.json](devto/articles/2263271.json)
  Evidence note: Links to a LinkedIn announcement of the move into a Developer Advocate role at MongoDB, marking a personal shift from relational databases toward NoSQL and document-database work.
- [2025: I'm joining MongoDB](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/2025-im-joining-mongodb-franck-pachot-e4shf) - 2025-02-06 | LinkedIn | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL, Amazon DynamoDB, Db2 | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security; archive: [linkedin/articles/e4shf.json](linkedin/articles/e4shf.json)
  Evidence note: Explains the move from distributed SQL advocacy to MongoDB DevRel as an opportunity to learn document modeling and help SQL developers evaluate database trade-offs without abandoning prior experience.
- [UUIDv7 in YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/uuidv7-in-yugabytedb-5ag7) - 2025-02-05 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/2259454.json](devto/articles/2259454.json)
  Evidence note: A custom uuid7() SQL function computes a time-ordered UUIDv7 from a timestamp and shift interval as a simple expression, avoiding recursive CTEs that trigger row-by-row buffering in YugabyteDB.
- [Where is the database schema? #SQL #NoSQL](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/where-database-schema-sql-nosql-franck-pachot-fleme) - 2025-01-31 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [linkedin/articles/fleme.json](linkedin/articles/fleme.json)
  Evidence note: Argues that every database has a schema: relational systems declare it centrally in tables, while document systems can distribute structure across documents, application code, and validation rules.
- [PostgreSQL 15 Compatibility in YugabyteDB 2.25: Top 15 Features!](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/postgresql-15-compatibility-in-yugabytedb/) - 2025-01-31 | Yugabyte | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, JSON & Documents; archive: [yugabyte/articles/33738.json](yugabyte/articles/33738.json)
  Evidence note: Runs gen_random_uuid(), generated columns, and other PostgreSQL 12-15 features live on YugabyteDB 2.25 preview to demonstrate concrete SQL compatibility beyond a version-number claim.
- [⛔ ORDER BY in Subquery](https://dev.to/yugabyte/order-by-in-subquery-3hop) - 2025-01-30 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/2250377.json](devto/articles/2250377.json)
  Evidence note: A two-table PostgreSQL example shows an ORDER BY inside a subquery has no effect on the outer query's row order, and OFFSET/LIMIT without an outer ORDER BY returns an effectively random subset.
- [set parameter if exists](https://dev.to/yugabyte/set-parameter-if-exists-3402) - 2025-01-29 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/2247779.json](devto/articles/2247779.json)
  Evidence note: An UPDATE against pg_settings filtered by version() LIKE '%-YB-%' sets a YugabyteDB-only parameter like yb_use_hash_splitting_by_default alongside application_name, harmless on plain PostgreSQL.
- [ESR (Equality, Sort, Range) rule applied to YugabyteDB and PostgreSQL indexes](https://dev.to/yugabyte/esr-equality-sort-range-rule-for-yugabytedb-indexes-fi4) - 2025-01-26 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/2241892.json](devto/articles/2241892.json)
  Evidence note: Responding to a LinkedIn claim that single-column indexes beat composite ones, MongoDB's Equality-Sort-Range indexing rule is shown to apply equally well to YugabyteDB and PostgreSQL indexes.
- [Indexing for NOT EQUAL (<>,!=) in YugabyteDB, PostgreSQL, Oracle Database, SQL Server, and MongoDB](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/indexing-for-not-equal-in-yugabytedb-postgresql-oracle-database-and-mongodb-25mo) - 2025-01-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB, Microsoft SQL Server | Query Optimization, Indexing, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2231411.json](devto/articles/2231411.json)
  Evidence note: Filtering WHERE value != 0 needs an index reading the two ranges around the excluded value; YugabyteDB's LSM-tree skip scan handles this better than most PostgreSQL, Oracle, or SQL Server setups.
- [PostgreSQL 15 Compatibility in YugabyteDB 2.25: Top 15 Features!](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/postgresql-15-compatibility-in-yugabytedb/) - 2025-01-22 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2231807.json](devto/articles/2231807.json)
  Evidence note: YugabyteDB 2.25 jumps from PostgreSQL 11 to full PostgreSQL 15 compatibility, adding core gen_random_uuid() without pgcrypto among fifteen features highlighted from the PG12-to-PG15 gap.
- [PostgreSQL Synchronized Sequential Scans and LIMIT without an ORDER BY](https://dev.to/franckpachot/postgresql-synchronized-sequential-scans-and-limit-without-an-order-by-1kia) - 2025-01-20 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/2227624.json](devto/articles/2227624.json)
  Evidence note: A background loop of concurrent sequential scans on a million-row table shows a repeatable-read SELECT * LIMIT 3 without ORDER BY returning different rows each time due to synchronization.
- [Aggregates with NULL: count(), min(), max(), sum()](https://dev.to/yugabyte/null-arithmetic-and-aggregates-count-min-max-sum-5c95) - 2025-01-17 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Migration & Compatibility, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2218295.json](devto/articles/2218295.json)
  Evidence note: Because NULL means unknown rather than zero, arithmetic like x+y+z with one NULL operand becomes unknown in YugabyteDB, yet aggregates like sum() and count() are defined to ignore NULL inputs.
- [What does NULL mean in an OUTER JOIN? ("unknown" vs. "not exists")](https://dev.to/yugabyte/what-does-null-mean-in-an-outer-join-unknown-vs-not-exists-4kng) - 2025-01-16 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2212775.json](devto/articles/2212775.json)
  Evidence note: Using the classic EMP/DEPT schema, distinguishes a NULL commission meaning an unknown-but-existing value from an OUTER JOIN's all-NULL row, including the key, meaning no matching row exists.
- [could not serialize access due to concurrent update](https://dev.to/yugabyte/could-not-serialize-access-due-to-concurrent-update-40na) - 2025-01-14 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/2206900.json](devto/articles/2206900.json)
  Evidence note: Explains YugabyteDB's 'could not serialize access' error by showing how read committed versus repeatable read/serializable isolation sets the consistent read-time snapshot differently.
- [PostgreSQL plan_cache_mode](https://dev.to/yugabyte/plancachemode-1jf9) - 2025-01-14 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/2207456.json](devto/articles/2207456.json)
  Evidence note: A prepared query against a 100,000-row table demonstrates YugabyteDB 2.25's plan_cache_mode, ported from PostgreSQL 12, choosing between a generic cached plan and a custom plan per execution.
- [Unique Index on NULL Values in SQL & NoSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/unique-index-on-null-values-in-sql-nosql-34ej) - 2025-01-12 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2193108.json](devto/articles/2193108.json)
  Evidence note: A telco call-record example compares three behaviors for unique indexes on NULL-or-missing values: MongoDB's document absence, PostgreSQL/YugabyteDB's SQL-standard NULL, and Oracle's variant.
- [Large IntentsDB MemTable with Many Small SST Files](https://dev.to/yugabyte/large-intentsdb-memtable-with-many-small-sst-files-pjl) - 2025-01-08 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Microsoft SQL Server | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/2194144.json](devto/articles/2194144.json)
  Evidence note: Contrasts SQL Server's Lock Escalation under transaction-intent memory pressure against distributed databases storing intents in a large MemTable with many small SST files on disk.
- [Index Filtering in PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB (Index Scan instead of Index Only Scan)](https://dev.to/yugabyte/index-filtering-in-postgresql-and-yugabytedb-1ck7) - 2025-01-03 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/2186701.json](devto/articles/2186701.json)
  Evidence note: Building on a case needing an Index Only Scan for an EXTRACT(TIMESTAMP) predicate, YugabyteDB can filter directly on Index Scan entries, making a smaller, non-covering index enough.
- [Aurora DSQL: How the Latest Distributed SQL Database Compares to YugabyteDB](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/aurora-dsql-compared-to-yugabytedb/) - 2025-01-02 | Yugabyte | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [yugabyte/articles/33701.json](yugabyte/articles/33701.json)
  Evidence note: Compares Aurora DSQL's disaggregated compute-storage architecture and single-region 99.99% SLA against YugabyteDB's PostgreSQL-code-reuse approach and multi-region consistency model.
- [Aurora DSQL: How it Compares to YugabyteDB](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/aurora-dsql-compared-to-yugabytedb) - 2025-01-02 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/2186347.json](devto/articles/2186347.json)
  Evidence note: Compares Aurora DSQL's PostgreSQL compatibility depth, architecture, concurrency control, and multi-region model against YugabyteDB, the earlier PostgreSQL-based distributed SQL database.

### 2024

- [More details in pg_locks for YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/more-information-in-pglocks-for-yugabytedb-vs-postgresql-7n8) - 2024-12-29 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2180360.json](devto/articles/2180360.json)
  Evidence note: Exaggerating a five-second DELETE to make locking observable, a blocked DELETE on the same rows appears in YugabyteDB's pg_locks with the blocking transaction ID visible in ybdetails.
- [Document data modeling to avoid write skew anomalies](https://dev.to/yugabyte/document-data-modeling-to-avoid-write-skew-anomaly-doctors-on-call-shift-example-465d) - 2024-12-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2178473.json](devto/articles/2178473.json)
  Evidence note: Storing doctors' on-call shifts in one JSONB column with SELECT FOR UPDATE reproduces the normalized two-table write-skew protection under read-committed isolation, without a second table.
- [When to replace IN() with EXISTS() - correlated and uncorrelated subqueries](https://dev.to/yugabyte/when-to-replace-in-with-exists-correlated-and-uncorrelated-subqueries-flk) - 2024-12-25 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Distributed Databases, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2174604.json](devto/articles/2174604.json)
  Evidence note: Using a users-and-messages schema with a nullable foreign key, execution plans for correlated versus uncorrelated subqueries show when rewriting IN() as EXISTS() actually changes the chosen plan.
- [Referential integrity In The Absence Of Foreign Key](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/referential-integrity-in-the-absence-of-foreign-key-3mbd) - 2024-12-19 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2165908.json](devto/articles/2165908.json)
  Evidence note: Since Aurora DSQL supports neither foreign keys nor serializable isolation, only application-side checks with careful concurrency handling remain viable among four referential-integrity strategies.
- [2024.2: Faster with Shared Memory Between PostgreSQL and TServer Layers](https://dev.to/yugabyte/20242-faster-with-shared-memory-between-postgresql-and-tserver-layers-5fg0) - 2024-12-17 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2160860.json](devto/articles/2160860.json)
  Evidence note: YugabyteDB 2024.2 makes pg_client_use_shared_memory generally available by default, replacing network calls between the PostgreSQL query layer and DocDB TServer with shared-memory buffers in tests.
- [Multi-Region Distributed SQL Transaction Latency](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/multi-region-distributed-sql-transaction-latency-512n) - 2024-12-16 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2159062.json](devto/articles/2159062.json)
  Evidence note: Deployed identically across three AWS regions, Aurora DSQL's optimistic-concurrency, no-pre-commit-sync design is compared against YugabyteDB's preferred-region replication for transaction latency.
- [Global Secondary Indexes in Distributed SQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/global-secondary-indexes-in-distributed-sql-database-47fm) - 2024-12-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Indexing, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2157413.json](devto/articles/2157413.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike sharded databases using only local indexes, Aurora DSQL and YugabyteDB both distribute secondary indexes independent of the table's primary-key sharding, enabling global unique constraints.
- [Optimistic Concurrency Control: Alice and Bob Couldn't Sit Together 🙁](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/optimistic-concurrency-control-alice-and-bob-couldnt-sit-together-5bh) - 2024-12-13 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/2155442.json](devto/articles/2155442.json)
  Evidence note: Alice and Bob try to book adjacent theater seats from a seat_row/seat_number table; tests on YugabyteDB's locking versus Aurora DSQL's optimistic concurrency show different outcomes.
- [Joins, Scale, and Denormalization](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/joins-and-denormalization-3dan) - 2024-12-11 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2151436.json](devto/articles/2151436.json)
  Evidence note: Comparing an orders/orderlines schema with added denormalized columns against a normalized join on Aurora DSQL answers whether distributed SQL still needs heavy denormalization to scale.
- [DynamoDB-style Limits for Predictable SQL Performance?](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/dynamodb-style-limits-for-predictable-performance-56b8) - 2024-12-11 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora, Amazon DynamoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2150524.json](devto/articles/2150524.json)
  Evidence note: Aurora DSQL needs only one synchronization per commit but can't flush unbounded-row DML in a single round; inserting a thousand YugabyteDB rows instead shows one 19.848ms flush for all writes.
- [Aurora DSQL - Simple Inserts Workload from an AWS CloudShell](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-dsql-simple-inserts-from-an-aws-cloudshell-2kkd) - 2024-12-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/2145951.json](devto/articles/2145951.json)
  Evidence note: Running the YugabyteDB sample-apps workload generator's single-row insert benchmark from an AWS CloudShell measures Aurora DSQL's read and write latency against each linked region's P99 network RTT.
- [Aurora DSQL: Create a Serverless Cluster and Connect with PostgreSQL Client](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-dsql-create-a-serverless-cluster-and-connect-with-postgresql-client-51mc) - 2024-12-07 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2134736.json](devto/articles/2134736.json)
  Evidence note: Opens a series testing AWS re:Invent 2024's newly previewed Aurora DSQL, clarifying it shares little architecture with regular RDS Aurora despite both using the standard PostgreSQL client.
- [Amazon Aurora DSQL: Which PostgreSQL Service Should I Use on AWS ?](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/amazon-aurora-dsql-which-postgresql-service-should-i-use-on-aws--1598) - 2024-12-03 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2122071.json](devto/articles/2122071.json)
  Evidence note: Weighs RDS PostgreSQL, RDS Aurora, Aurora Serverless v2, Aurora Limitless, Aurora DSQL, self-managed EC2, and YugabyteDB against each other by their PostgreSQL compatibility depth and scaling model.
- [Achieving Precise Clock Synchronization on AWS](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/aws-clock-synchronization/) - 2024-12-03 | Yugabyte | Oracle Database, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [yugabyte/articles/33645.json](yugabyte/articles/33645.json)
  Evidence note: Explains why YugabyteDB combines a Lamport logical clock with EC2 physical time, instead of a single monotonic counter like Oracle's SCN, to order transactions without a central bottleneck.
- [YugabyteDB MVCC and Updates: columns vs. JSON](https://dev.to/yugabyte/yugabytedb-mvcc-and-updates-columns-vs-json-3ndh) - 2024-12-01 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, JSON & Documents; archive: [devto/articles/2128846.json](devto/articles/2128846.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike Oracle's undo tablespace or PostgreSQL's in-table row versions, YugabyteDB's LSM-tree stores each column's MVCC versions under keys with a descending timestamp suffix, repacked at compaction.
- [Aurora Limitless - Sequences](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-sequences-3b74) - 2024-11-30 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2128232.json](devto/articles/2128232.json)
  Evidence note: On a two-shard Aurora Limitless cluster running pgbench_history without a primary key, sequence-generated identifiers still behave predictably once a sharding layer sits under PostgreSQL.
- [Aurora Limitless - Global Consistency (ACID)](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-global-consistency-acid-1m62) - 2024-11-29 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2118810.json](devto/articles/2118810.json)
  Evidence note: Zeroing every pgbench_accounts balance and transferring funds between shards shows Aurora Limitless keeps cross-shard reads strongly consistent, unlike Citus's eventually consistent reads.
- [Aurora Limitless - Collocation (and porting PgBench)](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-collocation-and-porting-pgbench-4jop) - 2024-11-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2118806.json](devto/articles/2118806.json)
  Evidence note: Running pgbench -i's table creation against Aurora Limitless fails with STORAGE PARAMETER errors, since fillfactor and similar PostgreSQL DDL options must be removed from the schema first.
- [Aurora Limitless - Reference Tables](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-reference-tables-1m9i) - 2024-11-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/2118768.json](devto/articles/2118768.json)
  Evidence note: Because an order_items table referencing a product catalog can't share the orders table's sharding key, limitless_create_table_mode=reference duplicates the small product table across all shards.
- [Aurora Limitless - SQL Limitations](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-sql-limitations-20if) - 2024-11-26 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2118557.json](devto/articles/2118557.json)
  Evidence note: Aurora Limitless shards PostgreSQL databases rather than running one distributed engine, so PARTITION BY on a sharded orders table fails since shards and partitions can't combine.
- [Aurora Limitless - Resharding](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-limitless-resharding-39dl) - 2024-11-26 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/2118660.json](devto/articles/2118660.json)
  Evidence note: Calling rds_aurora.limitless_split_shard on a 16-ACU shard group fails until capacity is raised, after which Aurora Limitless rebalances table slices onto the newly added shard automatically.

  Evidence note: Instead of serializable isolation to prevent a phantom read between reading max(num) and inserting max+1, a unique index on (type, num) forces the same no-gap serialization under read committed.
- [Better Than PostgreSQL! In-Place Index Updates with YugabyteDB](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/yugabytedb-in-place-index/) - 2024-11-25 | Yugabyte | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage; archive: [yugabyte/articles/33594.json](yugabyte/articles/33594.json)
  Evidence note: Shows how YugabyteDB's storage layer updates only the changed columns of a row instead of PostgreSQL's full-row-copy update, letting more indexes exist without added write overhead.

- [Covering Index nuances: which columns to cover (WHERE, ORDER BY, LIMIT, SELECT)?](https://www.linkedin.com/article/edit/7152619385403113472/) - 2024-01-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/1676787.json](devto/articles/1676787.json)
  Evidence note: Explores which columns belong in an index key versus an include clause for a multi-criteria orders query filtering on customer and quantity while sorting by quantity and date.
- [Repeatable Read with Wait-On-Conflict, and Restarts](https://dev.to/yugabyte/repeatable-read-with-wait-on-conflict-and-restarts-3fmp) - 2024-01-11 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/1723553.json](devto/articles/1723553.json)
  Evidence note: Reproduces a repeatable-read conflict error using a deliberately slow function to control timing across a ten-second full-table update, comparing outcomes across wait-queue settings.
- [Batched Nested Loop and Unbatchable Conditions](https://dev.to/yugabyte/batched-nested-loop-and-unbatchable-conditions-10n5) - 2024-01-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1722598.json](devto/articles/1722598.json)
  Evidence note: Explains how YugabyteDB's batched nested loop pushes a join condition down as an array of outer values, combining multiple columns with a row constructor for composite-key joins.
- ["Distributed Things" PostgreSQL](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/distributed-things-postgresql-franck-pachot-4fr7e) - 2024-01-09 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [linkedin/articles/4fr7e.json](linkedin/articles/4fr7e.json)
  Evidence note: Compares PostgreSQL extensions, forks, and distributed query-layer reuse as ways to add scalability while balancing compatibility, operational simplicity, and freedom to redesign storage and transactions.
- [UPSERT all columns in YugabyteDB (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE)](https://dev.to/yugabyte/upsert-all-columns-in-yugabytedb-on-conflict-do-update-4dpd) - 2024-01-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/1721367.json](devto/articles/1721367.json)
  Evidence note: Shows that upserting every column in YugabyteDB is faster as a delete-then-reinsert than a full update, because insert packs one storage entry per row while update writes one per column.
- [Isolation Levels - part XIII: Explicit Locking with SELECT (FOR UPDATE) intention](https://dev.to/yugabyte/isolation-levels-part-xiii-explicit-locking-with-select-for-update-intention-4na3) - 2024-01-05 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1716801.json](devto/articles/1716801.json)
  Evidence note: Traces explicit row locking back to SQL-92's updatable-cursor clause, arguing applications should declare their locking intent upfront since isolation levels alone don't cover every use case.
- [pg_hint_plan for Batched Nested Loop Join in YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/pghintplan-for-batched-nested-loop-join-in-yugabytedb-4pfo) - 2024-01-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/1708062.json](devto/articles/1708062.json)
  Evidence note: Warns that a newer YugabyteDB release turned batched nested loop into a full join method, so an older optimizer hint used to force it must be replaced with a differently named hint.

### 2023

- [Could Batched Nested Loop improve PostgreSQL like it does for YugabyteDB?](https://dev.to/yugabyte/could-batched-nested-loop-improve-postgresql-like-it-does-for-yugabytedb-4bjb) - 2023-12-23 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/1705699.json](devto/articles/1705699.json)
  Evidence note: Tests whether YugabyteDB's trick of replacing many individual index lookups with one array lookup would also help PostgreSQL, comparing values-joined and array-based execution plans.
- [`OR` Filter on Two Tables, and Batched Nested Loops](https://dev.to/yugabyte/or-filter-on-two-tables-and-batched-nested-loops-aa) - 2023-12-20 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1703388.json](devto/articles/1703388.json)
  Evidence note: Shows an OR filter spanning two hundred-thousand-row tables forcing a plain nested loop into thousands of inner lookups and several seconds, improved by a batched nested loop instead.
- [Single-statement deadlock in Oracle and ORA-00060](https://dev.to/franckpachot/single-statements-deadlocks-in-oracle-and-ora-00060-376m) - 2023-12-19 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1702816.json](devto/articles/1702816.json)
  Evidence note: Reproduces an Oracle single-statement deadlock using two differently ordered indexes and a slow function so two sessions update the same rows in reverse order, then asks if it can auto-retry.
- [Isolation Levels - part XII: To go further](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-xii-to-go-further-n89) - 2023-12-18 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/1675489.json](devto/articles/1675489.json)
  Evidence note: Links SQL anomaly test suites to an isolation-level critique and notes Oracle enforces foreign keys with an index structure that effectively behaves like a range lock.
- [Distributed PostgreSQL without Sharding constraint for SQL joins](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/distributed-postgresql-without-sharding-constraint-sql-franck-pachot-gjzne) - 2023-12-17 | LinkedIn | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [linkedin/articles/gjzne.json](linkedin/articles/gjzne.json)
  Evidence note: Argues that forcing hierarchical co-location and shard-key joins into distributed PostgreSQL sacrifices relational logical independence as applications gain new access paths and cross-shard relationships.
- [Isolation Levels - part XI: Read Uncommitted](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-xi-read-uncommitted-36fi) - 2023-12-13 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/1675488.json](devto/articles/1675488.json)
  Evidence note: Explains why read uncommitted exists only for SQL standard compatibility in non-MVCC databases, since PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB silently treat it exactly the same as read committed.
- [Isolation Levels - part X: Non-Transactional Writes](https://dev.to/yugabyte/isolation-levels-part-x-non-transactional-writes-1497) - 2023-12-13 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675487.json](devto/articles/1675487.json)
  Evidence note: Describes a session setting that skips the atomic-visibility step for bulk loads in YugabyteDB, comparing it to how Oracle and PostgreSQL already treat sequence updates as non-transactional.
- [ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING in YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/on-conflict-do-nothing-in-yugabytedb-991) - 2023-12-12 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1696053.json](devto/articles/1696053.json)
  Evidence note: Benchmarks an insert with a do-nothing conflict clause against a million-row table, showing YugabyteDB issues one read per candidate row and one flush per inserted row, taking milliseconds.
- [Even a Better Index 🍃 for The Most Annoying Postgres Optimizer Fail](https://dev.to/yugabyte/even-a-better-index-for-the-most-annoying-postgres-optimizer-fail-2ccg) - 2023-12-11 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MongoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/1694098.json](devto/articles/1694098.json)
  Evidence note: Improves on a prior best-index fix for a limit-one query by showing a loose index scan returns rows in the wrong order, then proposes a merge-append with union all to preserve ordering instead.
- [Isolation Levels - part IX: Read Committed](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-ix-read-committed-3lll) - 2023-12-10 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1675486.json](devto/articles/1675486.json)
  Evidence note: Compares read committed implementations across databases, noting Oracle lacks a share-locking select while PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB support shared and exclusive row locks with transparent restart.
- [Pagination with an OFFSET is better without OFFSET](https://dev.to/franckpachot/pagination-with-an-offset-is-better-without-offset-5fah) - 2023-12-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1691317.json](devto/articles/1691317.json)
  Evidence note: Extends a batched pagination example to show that adding an offset for the next page forces YugabyteDB to re-read and discard the first page's rows instead of resuming efficiently.
- [Extended Statistics and pg_hint_plan /*+ Rows() */](https://dev.to/yugabyte/extended-statistics-and-pghintplan-rows--j4k) - 2023-12-09 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/1693129.json](devto/articles/1693129.json)
  Evidence note: Loads a large cities table to show single-column selectivity estimates correctly, then demonstrates that correlated multi-column and cross-table predicates need both extended statistics and a hint.
- [The Most Annoying Optimizer Fail in Postgres ✅ Best index solved it.](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/the-most-annoying-optimizer-fail-in-postgres-solved-469b) - 2023-12-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Query Optimization, Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/1691860.json](devto/articles/1691860.json)
  Evidence note: Revisits a supposedly unfixable optimizer choice, showing a query filtering one column while sorting and limiting by another needs a genuinely better composite index rather than a workaround.
- [Isolation Levels - part VIII: Cursor Stability](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-viii-cursor-stability-40lm) - 2023-12-08 | Dev.to | Db2 | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675485.json](devto/articles/1675485.json)
  Evidence note: Explains a database-specific isolation level preventing lost updates by releasing read locks early, and shows locking selects or a single update-returning statement as MVCC equivalents.
- [Quick 📸 on 🐘 wait events from pg_stat_activity](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/quick-on-wait-events-from-pgstatactivity-4dkm) - 2023-12-07 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [devto/articles/1690005.json](devto/articles/1690005.json)
  Evidence note: Provides a psql script sampling pg_stat_activity many times over fifteen seconds and cross-tabulating wait-event counts across five time buckets, a lightweight session-history substitute.
- [Docker container with fixed IP address](https://dev.to/yugabyte/docker-container-with-fixed-ip-address-58cp) - 2023-12-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/1689438.json](devto/articles/1689438.json)
  Evidence note: Shows creating a Docker network with a fixed subnet and per-container static IP addresses so a lab cluster keeps stable node identities across container restarts.
- [Batched Nested Loop for Join With Large Pagination](https://dev.to/yugabyte/batched-nested-loop-for-join-with-pagination-621) - 2023-12-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Distributed Databases, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/1689768.json](devto/articles/1689768.json)
  Evidence note: Solves large-pagination joins where most driving-table rows have no matches, showing an order-preserving batched nested loop keeps response time bounded when the limit applies to the driving table.
- [Isolation Levels - part VII: Repeatable Read](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-vii-repeatable-read-l11) - 2023-12-05 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675484.json](devto/articles/1675484.json)
  Evidence note: Distinguishes scanned rows from fetched rows using a library book category example, showing snapshot isolation avoids locking while still permitting phantom reads by the strict SQL definition.
- [Isolation Levels - part VI: Snapshot Isolation](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-vi-snapshot-isolation-15k) - 2023-12-05 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1675483.json](devto/articles/1675483.json)
  Evidence note: Uses a two-managers-awarding-one-bonus-per-team example to illustrate write skew under snapshot isolation, noting Oracle's serializable setting actually only provides this weaker level.
- [How to Optimize Indexing for Distributed One-to-Many Join With Pagination](https://dev.to/yugabyte/how-to-optimize-indexing-for-distributed-one-to-many-join-with-pagination-4kcg) - 2023-12-05 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Amazon Aurora | Query Optimization, Indexing, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/1688704.json](devto/articles/1688704.json)
  Evidence note: Designs a hash-then-timestamp index with an included join column so a paginated join query against half a million rows responds in near-constant time regardless of table size.
- [Isolation Levels - part V: Read Only](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-v-read-only-323h) - 2023-12-04 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/1675482.json](devto/articles/1675482.json)
  Evidence note: Explains that marking a transaction READ ONLY lets MVCC systems serve a consistent snapshot without serializable conflict tracking, and YugabyteDB can read from stale local Raft followers.

  Evidence note: Explains that Oracle never implemented true serializable isolation despite the parameter name, while PostgreSQL's serializable snapshot isolation can still produce false-positive conflict errors.

- [PostgreSQL at AWS re:Invent](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/postgresql-at-aws-reinvent-378i) - 2023-12-01 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/1683411.json](devto/articles/1683411.json)
  Evidence note: An AWS re:Invent roundup highlights PostgreSQL's presence in general cloud sessions, especially managed-service forks where the platform's security boundary removes full host or superuser access.
- [Isolation Levels - part III: Main concepts](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-iii-main-concepts-55lg) - 2023-11-29 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675476.json](devto/articles/1675476.json)
  Evidence note: Introduces read time and write time as the core concepts behind isolation levels, contrasting multi-statement SQL transactions with single-object NoSQL operations that lack any serializability notion.
- [Atomic clocks in EC2](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/atomic-clocks-in-ec2-74h) - 2023-11-29 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/1682089.json](devto/articles/1682089.json)
  Evidence note: Summarizes a conference talk on a cloud time-sync service using GPS and atomic clocks with a hardware protocol to cut clock skew under a millisecond on select instance types and regions.

  Evidence note: Finds that PostgreSQL temporary tables bypass shared buffers entirely, relying on private memory and the OS filesystem cache, leaving under a fifth of RAM free for them on a large managed instance.
- [Isolation Levels - part II: Characteristics and use-case](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-ii-characteristics-and-use-case-21e0) - 2023-11-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675466.json](devto/articles/1675466.json)
  Evidence note: Maps serializable, repeatable read, read committed, and non-transactional writes to their read-time semantics and the OLTP use cases they fit across MVCC and non-MVCC databases.
- [PostgreSQL double buffering: understand the cache size in a managed service (OCI)](https://dev.to/franckpachot/postgresql-double-buffering-understand-the-cache-size-in-a-managed-service-oci-2oci) - 2023-11-27 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/1678023.json](devto/articles/1678023.json)
  Evidence note: Probes a managed PostgreSQL instance's double buffering by scanning a table growing in fixed increments, deriving the shared-buffer and filesystem-cache split from throughput changes.
- [Isolation Levels - part I: Introduction](https://dev.to/franckpachot/isolation-levels-part-i-introduction-bd5) - 2023-11-27 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1675465.json](devto/articles/1675465.json)
  Evidence note: Introduces isolation levels through a flight or hotel booking double-booking scenario, distinguishing physically serialized execution from the weaker but practical serializable isolation guarantee.
- [YBIO on OCI PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/franckpachot/ybio-on-oci-postgresql-cgc) - 2023-11-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/1676067.json](devto/articles/1676067.json)

  Evidence note: Contrasts monolithic SQL databases' coupled processes, shared memory, WAL, and files with distributed SQL's independently scalable query and sharded consensus-backed storage layers.

ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE, Read Committed Isolation Level and Lost Update](https://dev.to/yugabyte/dirty-writes-insert-on-conflict-do-update-read-committed-isolation-level-and-lost-update-13hk) - 2023-11-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/1660309.json](devto/articles/1660309.json)

  Evidence note: Notes PostgreSQL's ANALYZE has no command to remove gathered column statistics, and shows how to inspect and clear them manually while YugabyteDB 2.11's ANALYZE stays beta.

  Evidence note: Revisits a 2019 conference talk on Oracle features developers might miss, starting with lock-free SELECT under YugabyteDB's default SERIALIZABLE isolation versus optimistic locking.
- [Maintain a duplicate covering index in a region](https://dev.to/yugabyte/maintain-a-duplicate-covering-index-in-a-region-29k4) - 2022-01-18 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/957470.json](devto/articles/957470.json)
  Evidence note: Adds a duplicate covering index in a remote region so reads there stay fresh and local, accepting that writes now incur the added cross-region replication latency.

  Evidence note: Uses YugabyteDB's JDBC driver even when connecting to plain PostgreSQL, showing pessimistic locking makes concurrent token-bucket sessions wait rather than fail.

  Evidence note: Explains how the YugabyteDB JDBC Smart Driver extends the standard PostgreSQL driver so an app can connect to and load-balance across cluster nodes without a proxy.

  Evidence note: Starts a series implementing API rate limiting with a per-user SQL token-bucket counter as an alternative to an in-memory Redis counter unsuited to a distributed service.

  Evidence note: Finds trigger-maintained secondary tables outperform a GIN array index in YugabyteDB, because the GIN index's Rows Removed by Filter step is costly in distributed storage.

  Evidence note: Revisits a prior 'Oracle serializable is not serializable' example to show a basic PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB SERIALIZABLE transaction before a rate-limiter series.

### 2021

- [Multi-Cloud Distributed SQL: Avoiding Region Failure with YugabyteDB](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/avoiding-region-failure-with-multi-cloud-distributed-sql/) - 2021-12-22 | Yugabyte | YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [yugabyte/articles/5530.json](yugabyte/articles/5530.json)
  Evidence note: Distinguishes synchronous AZ replication from asynchronous cross-region replication in AWS, arguing multi-cloud, multi-region YugabyteDB deployments better survive full region failures.
- [🐘🚀 Triggers & Stored Procedures for pure data integrity logic and performance](https://dev.to/yugabyte/triggers-stored-procedures-for-pure-data-integrity-logic-and-performance-1eh8) - 2021-12-21 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/930622.json](devto/articles/930622.json)
  Evidence note: Answers a Slack question by designing a hybrid table for posts with tags and friend-group lists, balancing document simplicity against normalized relational access.
- [How to write SQL recursive CTE in 5 steps](https://dev.to/yugabyte/learn-how-to-write-sql-recursive-cte-in-5-steps-3n88) - 2021-12-17 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/928620.json](devto/articles/928620.json)
  Evidence note: Teaches WITH RECURSIVE by first joining the classic EMP table manually for two hierarchy levels, then converting the same logic into a recursive CTE run on YugabyteDB.
- [Quick 📸 on 🐘 active SQL from pg_stat_statements](https://dev.to/yugabyte/quick-on-active-sql-from-pgstatstatements-4e4h) - 2021-12-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/920737.json](devto/articles/920737.json)
  Evidence note: Provides a CTE query joining two pg_stat_statements snapshots taken 30 seconds apart via pg_sleep to compute delta activity, verified on YugabyteDB 2.9.
- [⏱ Read from the nearest peer in a multi-region database 🚀 with followers read](https://dev.to/franckpachot/read-from-the-nearest-peer-in-a-multi-region-database-with-followers-read-59d9) - 2021-12-07 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/918508.json](devto/articles/918508.json)
  Evidence note: Measures cross-region latency, 100 to 220 milliseconds, between three YugabyteDB regions, showing single-tablet reads under 1ms while multi-tablet reads take longer.
- ["Rows Removed by Index Recheck" on YugabyteDB Index Scan](https://dev.to/yugabyte/rows-removed-by-index-recheck-on-yugabytedb-index-scan-38aj) - 2021-12-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/918949.json](devto/articles/918949.json)
  Evidence note: Explains a since-fixed YugabyteDB Index Scan bug where the index condition acted as a lossy pre-filter returning extra rows, echoing PostgreSQL's lossy Bitmap block behavior.
- [🌎🌍🌏 Yugabyte cross-continent deployment 🚀](https://dev.to/yugabyte/yugabyte-cross-continent-deployment-b7i) - 2021-12-01 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/914416.json](devto/articles/914416.json)
  Evidence note: Deploys an RF=3 YugabyteDB cluster across AWS regions in Dublin, Ohio, and Mumbai, flagging Dublin as the preferred leader zone to bias write latency toward Europe.
- [Yugabyte COPY batch size](https://dev.to/yugabyte/yugabyte-copy-batch-size-25hh) - 2021-11-30 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/870474.json](devto/articles/870474.json)
  Evidence note: Explains why YugabyteDB's COPY defaults to committing every 1000 rows via yb_default_copy_from_rows_per_transaction, avoiding long Raft-consensus-backed transactions.
- [Text Search example with the OMDB sample database](https://dev.to/yugabyte/text-search-example-with-the-omdb-sample-database-5e4l) - 2021-11-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Microsoft SQL Server | Indexing, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/910840.json](devto/articles/910840.json)

  Evidence note: Notes early YugabyteDB versions lacked COPY progress visibility, later fixed via pg_stat_progress_copy, and shows yb_enable_upsert_mode for faster bulk loads.
- [Boost Secondary Index Queries with Index Only Scan](https://blog.yugabyte.com/how-a-distributed-sql-database-boosts-secondary-index-queries-with-index-only-scan/) - 2021-10-20 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases; archive: [devto/articles/870484.json](devto/articles/870484.json)
  Evidence note: Argues avoiding extra table-page hops via Index Only Scan is an overlooked optimization, noting PostgreSQL only gets this benefit on the already-vacuumed part of a table.
- [How a Distributed SQL Database Boosts Secondary Index Queries with Index Only Scan](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/how-a-distributed-sql-database-boosts-secondary-index-queries-with-index-only-scan/) - 2021-10-14 | Yugabyte | Database agnostic | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [yugabyte/articles/5096.json](yugabyte/articles/5096.json)
  Evidence note: Uses a customer-orders schema to show why covering secondary indexes let a distributed SQL database avoid the extra network hop back to the table on remote-node reads.
- [CREATE INDEX in YugabyteDB: online or fast?](https://dev.to/yugabyte/create-index-in-yugabytedb-online-or-fast-2dl3) - 2021-10-07 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/852920.json](devto/articles/852920.json)
  Evidence note: Contrasts YugabyteDB's fast CREATE INDEX, which share-locks the table against writes, with the slower online mode needing extra synchronization but avoiding blocking.
- [hyper-scale multi-tenant for SaaS: an example with pgbench](https://dev.to/yugabyte/hyper-scale-multi-tenant-for-saas-an-example-with-pgbench-33a3) - 2021-10-04 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Security, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/850175.json](devto/articles/850175.json)
  Evidence note: Demonstrates a maximally scalable SaaS multi-tenancy pattern: tenants share one schema and connection pool, isolated only by Row Level Security, tested on YugabyteDB.
- [The cost and benefit of synchronous replication in PostgreSQL and YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/the-cost-and-benefit-of-synchronous-replication-in-postgresql-and-yugabytedb-2ej0) - 2021-09-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/844066.json](devto/articles/844066.json)
  Evidence note: Explains why single-session throughput on one VM always favors monolithic PostgreSQL over YugabyteDB, since distributed durability relies on RPC replication, not a local write.
- [Bulk load into PostgreSQL / YugabyteDB - psycopg2](https://dev.to/yugabyte/bulk-load-into-postgresql-yugabytedb-psycopg2-fep) - 2021-09-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/816390.json](devto/articles/816390.json)
  Evidence note: Explains that psycopg2 lacks prepared statements, making per-row INSERT parsing inefficient for millions of rows, and shows using its COPY command for bulk loading.
- [when you have millions of insert statements in a file...](https://dev.to/yugabyte/when-you-have-millions-of-insert-statements-in-a-file-3nck) - 2021-09-22 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Security; archive: [devto/articles/836629.json](devto/articles/836629.json)
  Evidence note: Recounts a Telecom Billing system needing six large servers to process a thousand customers because it used a million literal INSERT statements instead of COPY loading.
- [YugabyteDB on OKE](https://blog.yugabyte.com/yugabytedb-on-oke-oracle-cloud-kubernetes/) - 2021-09-19 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/758157.json](devto/articles/758157.json)
  Evidence note: Documents installing YugabyteDB on a self-managed Oracle Cloud Kubernetes cluster built with Oracle's own Terraform Kubernetes installer, one of two OCI container routes.
- [Aurora PostgreSQL db.r6g compared to db.r5 with YBIO](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/aurora-postgresql-db-r6g-compared-to-db-r5-with-ybio-1bnd) - 2021-09-06 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/814368.json](devto/articles/814368.json)
  Evidence note: Uses the YBIO load generator to compare Aurora PostgreSQL 11.9 performance on an Intel Xeon db.r5.xlarge instance against the ARM Graviton2 db.r6g.xlarge equivalent.
- [YugabyteDB on OKE (Oracle Cloud Kubernetes)](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/yugabytedb-on-oke-oracle-cloud-kubernetes/) - 2021-09-03 | Yugabyte | Oracle Database, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Events & Community; archive: [yugabyte/articles/4719.json](yugabyte/articles/4719.json)
  Evidence note: Walks through provisioning a managed OKE Kubernetes cluster in Oracle Cloud, including VCN, load balancers, and node pools, to deploy YugabyteDB on Oracle infrastructure.
- [Index Scan in YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/index-scan-in-yugabytedb-5a2l) - 2021-09-03 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL | Query Optimization, Indexing, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/802855.json](devto/articles/802855.json)
  Evidence note: Clarifies that YugabyteDB's EXPLAIN output labeled 'Index Scan' on the primary index is really equivalent to Index Only Scan, since it already stores complete table rows.
- [UUID or cached sequences?](https://dev.to/yugabyte/uuid-or-cached-sequences-42fi) - 2021-08-30 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | General Database; archive: [devto/articles/807319.json](devto/articles/807319.json)
  Evidence note: Argues even a database without native sequence caching can mimic one by fetching, multiplying by 50, and counting locally, keeping sequences competitive with UUID keys.
- [Pressure Stall Information on CentOS7](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/pressure-stall-information-on-centos7-1700) - 2021-08-24 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/802266.json](devto/articles/802266.json)
  Evidence note: Installs a newer mainline kernel from ELRepo on CentOS 7, setting it as the default GRUB entry to enable Pressure Stall Information for CPU, RAM, and I/O.
- [Index Only Scan on Functional Indexes](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/index-only-scan-on-functional-indexes-9o2) - 2021-08-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MySQL | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/801683.json](devto/articles/801683.json)
  Evidence note: Shows a workaround adding redundant stored columns to the index or table so a functional index in PostgreSQL can still use Index Only Scan despite the visibility-map limit.
- [A kind of flashback query in PostgreSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/a-kind-of-flashback-query-in-postgresql-c02) - 2021-08-22 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | High Availability & Replication, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/800045.json](devto/articles/800045.json)
  Evidence note: Compares Oracle 9i's Flashback Query to a PostgreSQL equivalent achieved by leaving a REPEATABLE READ transaction open in one psql session while another commits changes.
- [pgbench --client --jobs](https://dev.to/yugabyte/pgbench-client-jobs-68g) - 2021-08-19 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/797140.json](devto/articles/797140.json)
  Evidence note: Clarifies pgbench's confusingly named --client option controls server-side connections while --jobs controls client-side threads generating the load.
- [usql on YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/usql-on-yugabytedb-2fk4) - 2021-08-17 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, Cassandra | Migration & Compatibility, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/794165.json](devto/articles/794165.json)
  Evidence note: Connects the multi-database usql client to YugabyteDB through the Go pq driver, showing the same protocol-compatible YSQL API works with tools built for psql.
- [Visible number of cpu on OpenVZ and LXC](https://dev.to/yugabyte/visible-number-of-cpu-on-openvz-2h7d) - 2021-08-05 | Dev.to | YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/782639.json](devto/articles/782639.json)
  Evidence note: Traces a YugabyteDB startup failure on Jelastic's OpenVZ/Virtuozzo virtualization to a percpu_rwlock assertion caused by mismatched Kubernetes and hypervisor CPU counts.
- [VisiData to read JSON, HTML and YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/visidata-to-read-json-html-and-yugabytedb-2ng1) - 2021-08-02 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability, JSON & Documents, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/778187.json](devto/articles/778187.json)
  Evidence note: Configures VisiData with psycopg2 and lxml so the terminal-based spreadsheet tool can browse a YugabyteDB database, JSON files, and HTML pages side by side.
- [PostgreSQL prepared statements in PL/pgSQL](https://dev.to/aws-heroes/postgresql-prepared-statements-in-pl-pgsql-jl3) - 2021-07-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Indexing; archive: [devto/articles/774581.json](devto/articles/774581.json)
  Evidence note: Runs a 10-million-iteration PL/pgSQL loop and measures that static SELECT statements are automatically prepared and fast, while equivalent dynamic SQL is not.
- [Arctype connects to YugabyteDB](https://blog.yugabyte.com/connecting-to-yugabytedb-with-arctype-a-collaborative-sql-client/) - 2021-07-28 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Migration & Compatibility; archive: [devto/articles/758912.json](devto/articles/758912.json)
  Evidence note: Walks through installing the Arctype collaborative SQL client on Windows and configuring its connection to query a YugabyteDB database over the PostgreSQL protocol.
- [SLOB on YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/slob-on-yugabytedb-1a32) - 2021-07-27 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/772358.json](devto/articles/772358.json)
  Evidence note: Argues against using application-simulating benchmarks like TPC-C to compare platforms, favoring SLOB's approach of a precisely known workload, applied here to YugabyteDB.
- [Connecting to YugabyteDB with Arctype, a Collaborative SQL Client](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/connecting-to-yugabytedb-with-arctype-a-collaborative-sql-client/) - 2021-07-23 | Yugabyte | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, Events & Community; archive: [yugabyte/articles/4425.json](yugabyte/articles/4425.json)
  Evidence note: Configures the Arctype collaborative SQL client against a YugabyteDB cluster, testing automatic reconnection across multiple IP addresses in a DNS entry for high availability.
- [YugabyteDB on OCI Free Tier](https://dev.to/yugabyte/yugabytedb-on-oci-free-tier-52cm) - 2021-07-21 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, YugabyteDB | Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Operations & Observability, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/754392.json](devto/articles/754392.json)
  Evidence note: Sets up YugabyteDB on a permanently free Oracle Cloud VM, 1/8 OCPU and 1GB RAM, from the Oracle Linux Cloud Developer image, opening the required database ports.
- [jOOQ on YugabyteDB](https://dev.to/yugabyte/jooq-on-yugabytedb-2kl9) - 2021-07-19 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/763515.json](devto/articles/763515.json)
  Evidence note: Writes a first jOOQ program against YugabyteDB on a 4-vCPU Arm Oracle Cloud instance, confirming no YugabyteDB-specific SQL dialect is needed since it reuses PostgreSQL.

  Evidence note: Contrasts a NoSQL collection's single hash-partitioning scheme with a normalized relational schema needing multiple tables and partitioning choices per access pattern.

  Evidence note: Compares PostgreSQL's default READ COMMITTED, using SELECT FOR UPDATE for repeatable reads on specific rows, to similar behavior long relied on in Oracle without retry logic.
- [EXPLAIN ANALYZE on COUNT() pushdown](https://dev.to/yugabyte/explain-analyze-on-count-pushdown-2978) - 2021-07-08 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Query Optimization, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage; archive: [devto/articles/751605.json](devto/articles/751605.json)
  Evidence note: Distinguishes offloading a COUNT() aggregation to YugabyteDB's DocDB storage layer, similar to Oracle Exadata's storage offload, from separately offloading reads to replicas.

  Evidence note: Uses pg_hint_plan to override single-table cardinality estimates in a query rather than through exact row counts, tested on a YugabyteDB table with no statistics gathered.
- [Reduce the complexity: be sharing and open source](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/yugbytedb-on-oci-oke/) - 2021-06-27 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/yugbytedb-on-oci-oke.json](dbi-services/articles/yugbytedb-on-oci-oke.json)
  Evidence note: Moving from RDBMS to NoSQL only reduces complexity when consistency requirements drop to single-item access, otherwise it shifts consistency work into application code instead.
- [See you on Polywork (an new LinkedIn?)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/see-you-on-polywork-an-new-linkedin/) - 2021-06-21 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/see-you-on-polywork-an-new-linkedin.json](dbi-services/articles/see-you-on-polywork-an-new-linkedin.json)

- [Cloud CLI](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/cloud-cli/) - 2021-05-30 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Cloud & Infrastructure, Security; archive: [dbi-services/articles/cloud-cli.json](dbi-services/articles/cloud-cli.json)
  Evidence note: Installing AWS, Google, Microsoft, and Oracle CLIs into a shared cloud directory, the AWS configure step favors text output over JSON because some AWS consoles have understated large consumed-capacity numbers.
- [Amazon RDS Oracle in Multitenant](https://blog.dbi-services.com/amazon-rds-oracle-in-multitenant/) - 2021-05-29 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/712288.json](devto/articles/712288.json)

- [An always free 4 vCPU 3.0 GHz 24 GB RAM on OCI](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-oci-arm-free/) - 2021-05-25 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-oci-arm-free.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-oci-arm-free.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Cloud's free tier added a 4 vCPU, 24GB RAM Ampere Altra ARM compute shape at 3.0GHz, always free like the two existing VMs, directly competing with AWS Graviton2's limited free trial.

  Evidence note: On AWS Graviton2, PostgreSQL only uses ARM v8.1 LSE atomic instructions when compiled with CFLAGS=-march=armv8-a+lse, since PostgreSQL's own configure script targets the older ARM v8 baseline by default.
- [Oracle Standard Edition on AWS ☁ socket arithmetic](https://blog.dbi-services.com/oracle-standard-edition-on-aws-a-socket-arithmetic/) - 2021-05-15 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/698748.json](devto/articles/698748.json)
  Evidence note: Clarifies May 2020 Oracle Standard Edition 2 licensing on AWS: every 4 vCPUs counts as one socket and two sockets count as one server, hyper-threading notwithstanding.
- [El Carro: The Oracle Operator for Kubernetes](https://blog.dbi-services.com/el-carro-the-oracle-operator-for-kubernetes/) - 2021-05-15 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security; archive: [devto/articles/698737.json](devto/articles/698737.json)
  Evidence note: Covers El Carro, Google's open-source Kubernetes Operator automating Oracle Database lifecycle operations, referencing the Google Open Source Blog announcement.

  Evidence note: Follows up a DynamoDB-to-relational terminology glossary by contrasting how 'index' means a sparse GSI/LSI structure in DynamoDB versus a partial B-tree index in Aurora.
- [An example of ORA-01152: file ... was not restored from a sufficiently old backup](https://blog.dbi-services.com/an-example-of-ora-01152-file-was-not-restored-from-a-sufficiently-old-backup/) - 2021-05-15 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery; archive: [devto/articles/698745.json](devto/articles/698745.json)
  Evidence note: Reproduces Oracle's ORA-01152 'file was not restored from a sufficiently old backup' error with a worked example showing the underlying media-recovery timeline state.

  Evidence note: Builds a glossary mapping Amazon DynamoDB's NoSQL API terms to their differently named relational equivalents, tracing how NoSQL evolved from replacing to complementing SQL.
- [AWS: PostgreSQL on Graviton2 with newer GCC](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2-aarch64/) - 2021-05-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/698746.json](devto/articles/698746.json)
  Evidence note: Finds PostgreSQL's default ./configure on AWS Graviton2 detects only armv8-a+crc, missing the ARMv8.1 LSE atomic instructions that matter for spinlock performance.
- [AWS Aurora vs. RDS PostgreSQL on frequent commits](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aws-aurora-vs-rds-postgresql/) - 2021-05-15 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/698750.json](devto/articles/698750.json)

  Evidence note: Delphix's 'Upgrade' button only repoints its Oracle Home reference after a manual database upgrade, so reverting a refreshed clone to an older Oracle version needs an undocumented telnet console command.
- [An example of ORA-01152: file … was not restored from a sufficiently old backup](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/an-example-of-ora-01152-file-was-not-restored-from-a-sufficiently-old-backup/) - 2021-04-30 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery; archive: [dbi-services/articles/an-example-of-ora-01152-file-was-not-restored-from-a-sufficiently-old-backup.json](dbi-services/articles/an-example-of-ora-01152-file-was-not-restored-from-a-sufficiently-old-backup.json)
  Evidence note: Reproducing ORA-01152 shows that RESETLOGS fails to open a database when a restored datafile's checkpoint predates the redo stream still required to bring it to a consistent state.

  Evidence note: Because PostgreSQL lacks Oracle-style session context variables or package globals, passing the acting user's identity into an audit trigger requires a session-scoped workaround instead of a built-in mechanism.
- [DynamoDB / Aurora: sparse and partial indexes](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-vs-aurora-vocabulary-sparse-and-partial-indexes/) - 2021-04-15 | dbi services | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora, Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-vs-aurora-vocabulary-sparse-and-partial-indexes.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-vs-aurora-vocabulary-sparse-and-partial-indexes.json)
  Evidence note: Loading a 25GB RNA dataset into Aurora PostgreSQL shows a covering index redundantly stores extra columns to avoid heap access, while a partial index excludes rows to keep the index itself smaller.

  Evidence note: Installs Oracle VirtualBox to run Kubernetes on a Windows laptop hosting YugabyteDB, showing a real scale-up-and-down SQL database rather than a Hello World demo.
- [K8s on Windows/VirtualBox](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/k8s-on-windows-virtualbox/) - 2021-04-12 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Development & Drivers; archive: [dbi-services/articles/k8s-on-windows-virtualbox.json](dbi-services/articles/k8s-on-windows-virtualbox.json)
  Evidence note: Running Minikube on Windows with the VirtualBox driver hosts a single-node Kubernetes cluster capable of scaling a real database's pods up and down while keeping it continuously available.
- [Some Artificial Intuition in Oracle SQL_ID?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/some-ai-in-oracle-sql_id/) - 2021-04-01 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Data Modeling & SQL, AI & Search; archive: [dbi-services/articles/some-ai-in-oracle-sql_id.json](dbi-services/articles/some-ai-in-oracle-sql_id.json)
  Evidence note: The Oracle SQL_ID hash generated for a query filtering EMP by JOB='PRESIDENT' on the SCOTT schema reproducibly comes out as 5c0tty145sddp, an odd resemblance to '5c0tt' in leetspeak.
- [Should CPU-intensive logic be done in the DB or in application server?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/should-cpu-intensive-logic-be-done-in-the-db-or-in-application-server/) - 2021-03-25 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [dbi-services/articles/should-cpu-intensive-logic-be-done-in-the-db-or-in-application-server.json](dbi-services/articles/should-cpu-intensive-logic-be-done-in-the-db-or-in-application-server.json)
  Evidence note: Running a Python ROT13 loop a million times through psycopg2 against a 100000-row PostgreSQL table shows database-side processing avoids the round-trip and context-switch cost of moving data to an app tier.
- [ORA-32635: not a single cell reference predicate](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/ora-32635-not-a-single-cell-reference-predicate/) - 2021-03-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Migration & Compatibility; archive: [dbi-services/articles/ora-32635-not-a-single-cell-reference-predicate.json](dbi-services/articles/ora-32635-not-a-single-cell-reference-predicate.json)
  Evidence note: ORA-32635 in a MODEL clause query traced back to NLS_COMP=LINGUISTIC set at instance level; switching the session to NLS_COMP=BINARY resolved the single-cell-reference-predicate error.
- [Linux perf-top basics: understand the %](https://dev.to/franckpachot/linux-perf-top-basics-understand-the-316l) - 2021-03-21 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [devto/articles/641379.json](devto/articles/641379.json)
  Evidence note: Explains how Linux's perf top samples CPU cycles and call stacks to build a per-function histogram, clarifying what its reported percentage columns represent.
- [Foreign Keys in MySQL, NoSQL, NewSQL (YugaByteDB)](https://dev.to/yugabyte/foreign-keys-in-mysql-sql-nosql-newsql-yugabytedb-3j0n) - 2021-03-21 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, MySQL | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [devto/articles/641382.json](devto/articles/641382.json)
  Evidence note: Traces the 'foreign keys are bad' folklore to MySQL's historically weak referential-integrity implementation, arguing it reflects a MySQL limitation, not SQL itself.
- [Foreign Keys in MySQL, SQL, NoSQL, NewSQL](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/foreign-keys-in-mysql-nosql-newsql/) - 2021-03-18 | dbi services | Oracle Database, MySQL | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [dbi-services/articles/foreign-keys-in-mysql-nosql-newsql.json](dbi-services/articles/foreign-keys-in-mysql-nosql-newsql.json)
  Evidence note: Testing MySQL 8.0.23 InnoDB foreign keys with sample countries and cities data shows an uncommitted parent-row update still blocks child-table DML, contradicting the claim that foreign keys don't scale.
- [Oracle – testing resource manager plans?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-testing-resource-manager-plans/) - 2021-03-16 | dbi services | Oracle Database | General Database; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-testing-resource-manager-plans.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-testing-resource-manager-plans.json)
  Evidence note: Without setting cpu_count or a Resource Manager Plan, 32 concurrent PL/SQL loop sessions on a 16-thread VM run purely on CPU with zero Resource Manager wait shown by Tanel Poder's Snapper script.
- [A VPC is a private cloud in a public cloud](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/a-vpc-is-a-private-cloud-in-a-public-cloud/) - 2021-03-15 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/a-vpc-is-a-private-cloud-in-a-public-cloud.json](dbi-services/articles/a-vpc-is-a-private-cloud-in-a-public-cloud.json)
  Evidence note: A cloud VPC in AWS and Google, Virtual Network in Azure, or VCN in Oracle plays the role of on-premises VLANs and subnets, while services like S3 bypass this virtual network layer entirely.
- [Linux perf-top basics: understand the %](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/linux-perf-top-basics-understand-the/) - 2021-03-14 | dbi services | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [dbi-services/articles/linux-perf-top-basics-understand-the.json](dbi-services/articles/linux-perf-top-basics-understand-the.json)
  Evidence note: Running perf top on two identical C functions called at a 1:3 versus 2:3 ratio confirms the reported Overhead percentage reflects relative sampled CPU time between symbols, not raw call counts.
- [AWS: PostgreSQL on Graviton2 with newer GCC](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2-aarch64/) - 2021-03-10 | dbi services | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2-aarch64.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2-aarch64.json)
  Evidence note: Objdump analysis of a Graviton2-compiled PostgreSQL binary shows LSE atomic instructions such as aarch64_swp4_acq already appear in XLogInsert and buffer-lock code without any explicit ARM v8.1 CFLAGS.
- [Pressure Stall Information on Autonomous Linux](https://blog.dbi-services.com/pressure-stall-information-on-autonomous-linux) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/629219.json](devto/articles/629219.json)
  Evidence note: Shows Pressure Stall Information is compiled into Oracle's Autonomous Linux kernel, based on OEL 7.9, but disabled by default, walking through checking and enabling it.
- [PostgreSQL on Linux: what is cached?](https://blog.dbi-services.com/postgresql-on-linux-what-is-cached/) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/629218.json](devto/articles/629218.json)
