  Evidence note: Notes PostgreSQL's default pgbench scale stores about 15MB per scale unit mostly in pgbench_accounts, arguing TPS figures are meaningless without knowing the cache layer served.
- [Grafana, SQL and IN() list for multi-value variable](https://blog.dbi-services.com/grafana-sql-and-in-list-for-multi-value-variable) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/629217.json](devto/articles/629217.json)
  Evidence note: Works around a Grafana limitation building a SQL IN() clause from a multi-value dashboard variable, noting the underlying GitHub issue remained unresolved.
- [DynamoDB Scan: the most efficient operation 😉](https://blog.dbi-services.com/dynamodb-scan-the-most-efficient-operation-%f0%9f%98%89/) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | Amazon DynamoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [devto/articles/629222.json](devto/articles/629222.json)
  Evidence note: Provocatively argues that blanket advice to avoid DynamoDB Scan operations can push developers toward workarounds performing even worse than a well-understood Scan.
- [Amazon Aurora: calling a lambda from a trigger](https://blog.dbi-services.com/amazon-aurora-calling-a-lambda-from-a-trigger) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | MySQL, Amazon Aurora | High Availability & Replication, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/629224.json](devto/articles/629224.json)
  Evidence note: Documents calling mysql.lambda_async() from an Amazon Aurora MySQL trigger to push a notification to AWS Lambda on data change, cautioning about its async behavior.
- [AWS: PostgreSQL on Graviton2](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2/) - 2021-03-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/629216.json](devto/articles/629216.json)
  Evidence note: Covers running PostgreSQL on the free-tier AWS T4g.micro Graviton2 instance, available until June 2021 alongside the standard 750-hour t2.micro free tier.
- [AWS: PostgreSQL on Graviton2](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2/) - 2021-03-08 | dbi services | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-postgresql-on-graviton2.json)
  Evidence note: Compiling PostgreSQL 14devel from community source on an m6gd.2xlarge Graviton2 instance versus an m5d.2xlarge x86_64 instance shows near-identical build time before running the PGIO benchmark.
- [Delphix: a glossary to get started](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/delphix-a-glossary-to-get-started/) - 2021-03-02 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Internals & Storage; archive: [dbi-services/articles/delphix-a-glossary-to-get-started.json](dbi-services/articles/delphix-a-glossary-to-get-started.json)
  Evidence note: Delphix's setup console runs a storage duration test before engine initialization and uses commands like ls, commit, and up on a keyboard layout where quote and equals keys sit on the numeric pad.
- [Oracle Rolling Invalidate Window Exceeded(3)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-rolling-invalidate-window-exceeded3/) - 2021-02-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-rolling-invalidate-window-exceeded3.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-rolling-invalidate-window-exceeded3.json)
  Evidence note: Setting the hidden parameter _optimizer_invalidation_period to 15 seconds instead of the 5-hour default confirms DBA_TAB_STATS_HISTORY and cursor KSUGCTM timestamps drive Oracle's rolling cursor invalidation.
- [Oracle DB on Azure with Multitenant Option](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-db-on-azure-with-multitenant-option/) - 2021-02-22 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-db-on-azure-with-multitenant-option.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-db-on-azure-with-multitenant-option.json)
  Evidence note: Running Oracle 19c on an Azure marketplace VM image requires manually fetching OPatch and Release Update binaries from Azure instance metadata tags before applying them through CustomData scripts.
- [Grafana, SQL and IN() list for multi-value variable](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/grafana-sql-and-in-list-for-multi-value-variable/) - 2021-02-18 | dbi services | PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/grafana-sql-and-in-list-for-multi-value-variable.json](dbi-services/articles/grafana-sql-and-in-list-for-multi-value-variable.json)
  Evidence note: Grafana's multi-value template variable builds a SQL IN() list from user selections, requiring __value and __text column aliases so the dashboard shows country names while filtering by country_id.
- [PostgreSQL on Linux: what is cached?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/postgresql-on-linux-what-is-cached/) - 2021-02-14 | dbi services | PostgreSQL | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/postgresql-on-linux-what-is-cached.json](dbi-services/articles/postgresql-on-linux-what-is-cached.json)
  Evidence note: Linux's free command reports shared_buffers pages twice, once as filesystem cache and once as shared memory, so a table cached in both shows roughly double the on-disk dataset size as 'cached'.
- [Learn ODA on Oracle Cloud](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/odasim/) - 2021-02-03 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/odasim.json](dbi-services/articles/odasim.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's free ODA Simulator, deployed from the Oracle Cloud Marketplace, uses Portainer over Docker so createOdaSimulatorContainer.sh can spin up HA or lite-class ODA containers for command-line practice.
- [Google Cloud SQL Insights: ASH, plans and statement tagging](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/google_insights/) - 2021-01-31 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/google_insights.json](dbi-services/articles/google_insights.json)
  Evidence note: Google Cloud SQL Insights samples PostgreSQL wait events for CPU, I/O, and lock contention every minute with 7-day retention, mirroring the ASH sampling model long used by Oracle AWR and RDS Performance Insights.
- [19c serverless logon trigger](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/19c-serverless-logon-trigger/) - 2021-01-31 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/19c-serverless-logon-trigger.json](dbi-services/articles/19c-serverless-logon-trigger.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's SESSION_SETTINGS clause inside a client connection string, such as (SESSION_SETTINGS=(optimizer_mode=first_rows_10)), applies ALTER SESSION parameters at connect time without needing a logon trigger.
- [Pressure Stall Information on Autonomous Linux](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/pressure-stall-information-on-autonomous-linux/) - 2021-01-09 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/pressure-stall-information-on-autonomous-linux.json](dbi-services/articles/pressure-stall-information-on-autonomous-linux.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Autonomous Linux ships a kernel compiled with PSI support but disabled by default, so enabling Pressure Stall Information requires adding psi=1 to the boot command line through a custom tuned profile.
- [Google Spanner – SQL compatibility](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/google-spanner-sql-compatibility/) - 2021-01-05 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/google-spanner-sql-compatibility.json](dbi-services/articles/google-spanner-sql-compatibility.json)
  Evidence note: Google Cloud Spanner gained a NUMERIC data type since an earlier evaluation, improving SQL compatibility for a globally distributed relational database provisioned here through the gcloud command line.

### 2020

- [Optimizer Statistics Gathering – pending and history](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/optimizer-statistics-gathering-pending-and-history/) - 2020-12-29 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/optimizer-statistics-gathering-pending-and-history.json](dbi-services/articles/optimizer-statistics-gathering-pending-and-history.json)
  Evidence note: Setting DBMS_STATS.SET_TABLE_PREFS('PUBLISH','FALSE') lets freshly gathered statistics stay pending without immediately affecting the optimizer, avoiding a sudden E-Rows versus A-Rows plan mismatch.
- [Password rolling change before Oracle 21c](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/password-rolling-change-before-oracle-21c/) - 2020-12-27 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/password-rolling-change-before-oracle-21c.json](dbi-services/articles/password-rolling-change-before-oracle-21c.json)
  Evidence note: Before 21c's Gradual Password Rollover feature, a proxy user with CONNECT THROUGH lets an application authenticate with a yearly-rotated credential while the actual schema owner's password stays unused.
- [DynamoDB Scan: the most efficient operation 😉](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-scan-the-most-efficient-operation-%f0%9f%98%89/) - 2020-12-20 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-scan-the-most-efficient-operation-%f0%9f%98%89.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-scan-the-most-efficient-operation-%f0%9f%98%89.json)
  Evidence note: Scanning a 5000-item DynamoDB table with a COUNT selection consumes only 6 RCU total, showing a full table Scan can cost less per item than issuing 5000 separate single-key GetItem calls.
- [Oracle SPD status on two learning paths](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-spd-status-on-two-learning-paths/) - 2020-12-19 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-spd-status-on-two-learning-paths.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-spd-status-on-two-learning-paths.json)
  Evidence note: Bug 20311655 turns out to be expected behavior: a SQL Plan Directive reaches the HAS_STATS learning state and repeats a misestimate whenever extended statistics cannot resolve the correlated predicate.
- [Efficiently query DBA_EXTENTS for FILE_ID / BLOCK_ID](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/efficiently-query-dba_extents-for-file_id-block_id/) - 2020-12-15 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Internals & Storage; archive: [dbi-services/articles/efficiently-query-dba_extents-for-file_id-block_id.json](dbi-services/articles/efficiently-query-dba_extents-for-file_id-block_id.json)
  Evidence note: Rewriting a DBA_EXTENTS lookup to start from X$KCCFE and X$KTFBUE instead of DBA_SEGMENTS located a corrupt block's owning segment on a 900TB, 7000-datafile database in about one second.
- [Oracle write consistency bug and multi-thread de-queuing](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-write-consistency-bug-and-multi-thread-de-queuing/) - 2020-12-14 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-write-consistency-bug-and-multi-thread-de-queuing.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-write-consistency-bug-and-multi-thread-de-queuing.json)
  Evidence note: A 2011 Oracle bug, still reproducible in 18c, lets two concurrent UPDATE statements dequeuing the same 5 oldest 'NEW' rows both end up updating the identical set instead of five distinct rows each.
- [Amazon Aurora: calling a lambda from a trigger](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/amazon-aurora-calling-a-lambda-from-a-trigger/) - 2020-12-14 | dbi services | MySQL, Amazon Aurora | High Availability & Replication, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/amazon-aurora-calling-a-lambda-from-a-trigger.json](dbi-services/articles/amazon-aurora-calling-a-lambda-from-a-trigger.json)
  Evidence note: Aurora MySQL's mysql.lambda_async() call lets a trigger invoke a Lambda function on any DML, so an unencapsulated AFTER UPDATE trigger risks firing unwanted notifications during bulk fixes or replication replay.
- [Recovery in the ☁ with Google Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL)](https://blog.dbi-services.com/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-google-cloud-sql-postgresql/) - 2020-12-11 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/539983.json](devto/articles/539983.json)
  Evidence note: Continues a cloud-recovery series started with Oracle Autonomous Database by clarifying Point-In-Time-Recovery procedures specific to Google Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL.
- [Recovery in the ☁ with Google Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-google-cloud-sql-postgresql/) - 2020-12-11 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, MySQL | Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-google-cloud-sql-postgresql.json](dbi-services/articles/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-google-cloud-sql-postgresql.json)
  Evidence note: Google Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL treats a 'backup' as only a restore point, so continuous WAL-based recovery to any moment since the last backup requires separately enabling point-in-time recovery.
- [Aurora Serverless v2 (preview) - RAM](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aurora-serverless-v2-ram) - 2020-12-08 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/536935.json](devto/articles/536935.json)
  Evidence note: Explains that Aurora Capacity Units combine CPU and RAM, contrasting Serverless v1's power-of-two scaling from 1 to 256 ACU, with scale-to-zero, against v2's finer model.
- [Aurora Serverless v2 (preview) - CPU](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aurora-serverless-v2-cpu/) - 2020-12-08 | Dev.to | Amazon Aurora | Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/536936.json](devto/articles/536936.json)
  Evidence note: Follows up a RAM auto-scaling test by measuring CPU Utilization on an Aurora Serverless v2 preview database configured to auto-scale from 4 to 32 ACU during a table scan.
- [Aurora Serverless v2 (preview) – RAM](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aurora-serverless-v2-ram/) - 2020-12-07 | dbi services | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Migration & Compatibility, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aurora-serverless-v2-ram.json](dbi-services/articles/aurora-serverless-v2-ram.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike Serverless v1's power-of-two scale-up that stops and restarts the instance, Aurora Serverless v2 preview resizes RAM and the buffer pool online in finer ACU increments without dropping connections.
- [Aurora Serverless v2 (preview) – CPU](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aurora-serverless-v2-cpu/) - 2020-12-07 | dbi services | Amazon Aurora | Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aurora-serverless-v2-cpu.json](dbi-services/articles/aurora-serverless-v2-cpu.json)
  Evidence note: Aurora Serverless v2 preview scaled from 6 ACU at 14% CPU with one session to 32 ACU at 97% CPU with eight concurrent sessions, implying the engine underneath runs on roughly an 8 vCPU host.
- [Database announcements at re:Invent 2020](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/database-announcements-at-reinvent-2020/) - 2020-12-03 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL, Amazon Aurora | High Availability & Replication, Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/database-announcements-at-reinvent-2020.json](dbi-services/articles/database-announcements-at-reinvent-2020.json)
  Evidence note: AWS re:Invent 2020 introduced Babelfish for Aurora, Aurora Serverless v2, and Glue Elastic Views, alongside RDS Proxy connection pooling and Graviton2 processor support for RDS PostgreSQL and MySQL.
- [DynamoDB Scan (and why 128.5 RCU?)](https://blog.dbi-services.com/dynamodb-scan-pagination/) - 2020-11-30 | Dev.to | Amazon DynamoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/528717.json](devto/articles/528717.json)
  Evidence note: Follows up a PartiQL SELECT post to explain why an unfiltered DynamoDB Scan reported 128.5 read capacity units and how the regular API automatically paginates results.
- [DynamoDB Scan (and why 128.5 RCU?)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-scan-pagination/) - 2020-11-30 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-scan-pagination.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-scan-pagination.json)
  Evidence note: A DynamoDB Scan over roughly 5.2MB of 5000 items with 1042-byte items consumes 128.5 RCU per eventually-consistent page, because each 1MB read unit is billed even when items only partly fill it.
- [PMM Server on AWS: which TCP ports to open](https://blog.dbi-services.com/cross-cloud-pmm-which-tcp-ports-to-open) - 2020-11-29 | Dev.to | PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/528263.json](devto/articles/528263.json)
  Evidence note: Installs Percona Monitoring and Management from the AWS Marketplace AMI and documents which TCP ports must be opened to monitor a database hosted on a separate cloud.
- [Cross-cloud PMM: which TCP ports to open](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/cross-cloud-pmm-which-tcp-ports-to-open/) - 2020-11-29 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/cross-cloud-pmm-which-tcp-ports-to-open.json](dbi-services/articles/cross-cloud-pmm-which-tcp-ports-to-open.json)
  Evidence note: Monitoring an OCI Autonomous Linux instance from a Percona PMM server hosted on AWS requires opening the HTTPS port for the client exporter's traffic to cross between the two cloud providers.
- [Amazon DynamoDB Local: running NoSQL on SQLite](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aws-dynamodb-local/) - 2020-11-26 | Dev.to | Amazon DynamoDB, SQLite | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/526025.json](devto/articles/526025.json)
  Evidence note: Describes DynamoDB Local's simple Get/Put/Query/Scan API and hash-partitioned design, noting its local implementation actually stores data inside an SQLite file.
- [What is a database backup (back to the basics)](https://blog.dbi-services.com/what-is-a-database-backup-back-to-the-basics) - 2020-11-25 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/525153.json](devto/articles/525153.json)
  Evidence note: Argues logical dumps such as pg_dump or Oracle expdp should not be considered database backups, and no backup should be trusted until recovery has been tested.
- [SQL101 SQL Basics (NoSQL to SQL) all on Oracle Autonomous Database](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-eL-G-9cLVk&ab_channel=FranckPachot) - 2020-11-25 | Dev.to | Oracle Database | JSON & Documents, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/524561.json](devto/articles/524561.json)
  Evidence note: Shares a DOAG conference recording, replayable at sql101.pachot.net, walking through SQL basics on Oracle Autonomous Database starting from a NoSQL mindset.
- [DynamoDB PartiQL – part II: SELECT](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-partiql-part-ii-select/) - 2020-11-25 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-partiql-part-ii-select.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-partiql-part-ii-select.json)
  Evidence note: DynamoDB's PartiQL SELECT still resolves to GetItem, Query, or Scan depending on whether the WHERE clause has an equality predicate on the partition key, so the underlying physical cost is unchanged.
- [DynamoDB PartiQL - part II: SELECT](https://blog.dbi-services.com/dynamodb-partiql-part-ii-select) - 2020-11-25 | Dev.to | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [devto/articles/524563.json](devto/articles/524563.json)
  Evidence note: Shows that DynamoDB's PartiQL SELECT translates SQL-like syntax into calls to the existing NoSQL API with no added query engine, so ORDER BY support stays limited.
- [PostgreSQL in AWS: clearing the doubts](https://blog.dbi-services.com/postgresql-in-aws-clearing-the-doubts/) - 2020-11-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL, Amazon Aurora | Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Events & Community; archive: [devto/articles/524270.json](devto/articles/524270.json)
  Evidence note: Rebuts the claim that AWS's managed PostgreSQL isn't the real community edition, contrasting it with self-managed EC2 installs allowing any version or extension.
- [DynamoDB PartiQL – part I: INSERT](https://blog.dbi-services.com/dynamodb-partiql-i/) - 2020-11-24 | Dev.to | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [devto/articles/524266.json](devto/articles/524266.json)
  Evidence note: Introduces DynamoDB's new PartiQL INSERT syntax as a SQL-like layer added atop AWS's purpose-built NoSQL services, which otherwise share no common query language.
- [DynamoDB PartiQL – part I: INSERT](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-partiql-i/) - 2020-11-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-partiql-i.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-partiql-i.json)
  Evidence note: DynamoDB's new PartiQL INSERT, SELECT, UPDATE, and DELETE syntax, tested here through the AWS CLI's execute-statement command, still stores single items with no joins and no multi-item ACID transactions.
- [AWS burstable EC2 and CloudWatch metrics](https://blog.dbi-services.com/aws-burstable-ec2-cloudwatch/) - 2020-11-24 | Dev.to | Database agnostic | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [devto/articles/524267.json](devto/articles/524267.json)
  Evidence note: Explains why undersized burstable EC2 instances risk performance issues at peak load, walking through CPU credit consumption and the CloudWatch 'stolen' CPU metric.
- [AWS burstable EC2 and CloudWatch metrics](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-burstable-ec2-cloudwatch/) - 2020-11-23 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-burstable-ec2-cloudwatch.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-burstable-ec2-cloudwatch.json)
  Evidence note: A t2.micro EC2 instance's CloudWatch CPU Credit Balance drops from 32 minutes to zero during a sustained 100% CPU process, after which CPU Utilization throttles down to a roughly 20% baseline.
- [Recovery in the ☁ with Oracle Autonomous Database](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-oracle-autonomous-database/) - 2020-11-18 | dbi services | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-oracle-autonomous-database.json](dbi-services/articles/recovery-in-the-%e2%98%81-with-oracle-autonomous-database.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Autonomous Database's point-in-time recovery always creates a new cloned database from a chosen timestamp or backup instead of restoring in place, preserving the original database's later transactions.
- [AWS DynamoDB -> S3 -> OCI Autonomous Database](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-dynamodb-s3-oci-autonomous-database/) - 2020-11-16 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-s3-oci-autonomous-database.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-s3-oci-autonomous-database.json)
  Evidence note: AWS's no-code DynamoDB-to-S3 export combined with Oracle Autonomous Database's external table support lets Oracle SQL query DynamoDB-exported JSON files directly from an S3-compatible object store.
- [PostgreSQL in AWS: clearing the doubts](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/postgresql-in-aws-clearing-the-doubts/) - 2020-11-09 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL, Amazon Aurora | Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/postgresql-in-aws-clearing-the-doubts.json](dbi-services/articles/postgresql-in-aws-clearing-the-doubts.json)
  Evidence note: Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL runs the same community-compiled PostgreSQL binaries you would build yourself on EC2, distinguishing it from Aurora, a separate proprietary storage engine sharing only the RDS family name.
- [NoSQL and SQL: key-value access always scale](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/key-value-access-always-scale/) - 2020-11-04 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/key-value-access-always-scale.json](dbi-services/articles/key-value-access-always-scale.json)
  Evidence note: A 4TB Oracle table left unpurged for years and split across 30 million extents still performed acceptably for end users, because hash-partitioned key-value access scales independently of total data size.
- [What is a database backup (back to the basics)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/what-is-a-database-backup-back-to-the-basics/) - 2020-10-22 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Backup & Recovery; archive: [dbi-services/articles/what-is-a-database-backup-back-to-the-basics.json](dbi-services/articles/what-is-a-database-backup-back-to-the-basics.json)
  Evidence note: A logical export like pg_dump or Oracle expdp is not a database backup, because it alone cannot support point-in-time recovery to any moment between the last export and a later failure.
- [YCSB (NoSQL benchmark) on Oracle Database](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/ycsb-nosql-benchmark-on-oracle-database/) - 2020-10-12 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Cassandra | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/ycsb-nosql-benchmark-on-oracle-database.json](dbi-services/articles/ycsb-nosql-benchmark-on-oracle-database.json)
  Evidence note: The Yahoo Cloud Serving Benchmark, designed around a simple key-value get/put NoSQL workload generator, can also drive comparable load against Oracle Database through its extensible client interface.
- [CLUSTER](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/cluster/) - 2020-10-07 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/cluster.json](dbi-services/articles/cluster.json)
  Evidence note: RAC clusters multiple Oracle instances together for higher availability, referenced here only as a light analogy for a 2014 Oracle Open World dinner that gathered several long-time database community members.
- [Oracle ADB from a Jupyter Notebook](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-adb-jupyter/) - 2020-09-30 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-adb-jupyter.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-adb-jupyter.json)
  Evidence note: Connecting a Google Colab Jupyter notebook to Oracle Autonomous Database on the Free Tier requires installing libaio1, the Instant Client, SQLcl, and a public-bucket wallet through wget and unzip commands.
- [Oracle MAA reference architecture and HA, DR, RTO, RPO](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-maa-reference-architecture-and-ha-dr-rto-rpo/) - 2020-09-26 | dbi services | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Backup & Recovery; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-maa-reference-architecture-and-ha-dr-rto-rpo.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-maa-reference-architecture-and-ha-dr-rto-rpo.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's Maximum Availability Architecture ranks Bronze (single instance with RMAN), Silver (RAC), Gold (RAC plus synchronous Data Guard), and Platinum (adding GoldenGate and sharding) by product combination.
- [Amazon Aurora Serverless (PostgreSQL compatibility)](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/amazon-aurora-serverless-postgresql-compatibility/) - 2020-09-20 | dbi services | PostgreSQL, MySQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [dbi-services/articles/amazon-aurora-serverless-postgresql-compatibility.json](dbi-services/articles/amazon-aurora-serverless-postgresql-compatibility.json)
  Evidence note: Amazon Aurora Serverless supported only PostgreSQL 10.7 compatibility at the time, auto-pausing to zero capacity after 5 minutes idle and resuming through a VPC-only proxy endpoint with no public access.
- [What is Object Storage?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/what-is-object-storage/) - 2020-09-18 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/what-is-object-storage.json](dbi-services/articles/what-is-object-storage.json)
  Evidence note: Object storage exposes flat buckets of UUID-identified objects carrying rich metadata tags instead of a directory hierarchy, unlike the file and block-device model that databases traditionally read from.
- [Amazon or AWS services?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/amazon-or-aws-services/) - 2020-09-18 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Backup & Recovery, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/amazon-or-aws-services.json](dbi-services/articles/amazon-or-aws-services.json)
  Evidence note: AWS names standalone customer-facing services 'Amazon', like DynamoDB and RDS, while utility services operating on them, like Backup or Database Migration Service, are named 'AWS' per community naming convention.
- [Amazon DynamoDB: a r(el)ational Glossary](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-dynamodb-a-relational-glossary/) - 2020-09-17 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-a-relational-glossary.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-a-relational-glossary.json)
  Evidence note: In DynamoDB an Attribute is a JSON member with no fixed domain or length limit, unlike a relational column, and its partition plus optional sort key together replace a composite primary key's ordering role.
- [Troubleshooting performance on Autonomous Database](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/troubleshooting-autonomous-database/) - 2020-08-29 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/troubleshooting-autonomous-database.json](dbi-services/articles/troubleshooting-autonomous-database.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Autonomous Database's Performance Hub drills ASH activity by Consumer Group, Wait Class, and Wait Event, isolating roughly 0.86 average active sessions attributed entirely to internal background processes.
- [Data virtualization on SQL Server with Redgate SQL Clone](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/data-virtualization-on-sql-server-with-redgate-sql-clone/) - 2020-08-25 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Microsoft SQL Server | Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/data-virtualization-on-sql-server-with-redgate-sql-clone.json](dbi-services/articles/data-virtualization-on-sql-server-with-redgate-sql-clone.json)
  Evidence note: Redgate SQL Clone builds a detached 'Image' directly from a SQL Server .bak backup file, letting several thin clones share the same underlying data blocks instead of each restoring a full copy.
- [SQL Server on Oracle Cloud](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/sql-server-on-oracle-cloud/) - 2020-08-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Microsoft SQL Server | Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/sql-server-on-oracle-cloud.json](dbi-services/articles/sql-server-on-oracle-cloud.json)
  Evidence note: Deploying SQL Server 2016 Standard from the Oracle Cloud Marketplace requires opening ingress ports 3389 for RDP and 1433 for the SQL Server listener, since it runs as an IaaS VM rather than a managed PaaS service.
- [Oracle ADB: rename the service_name connect_data](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-adb-rename/) - 2020-08-13 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Security, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-adb-rename.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-adb-rename.json)
  Evidence note: Since August 2020, an Oracle Autonomous Database instance can be renamed, which changes the service_name embedded in the CONNECT_DATA entry that existing client wallets use to reach the underlying PDB.
- [Amazon DynamoDB: the cost of indexes](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-dynamodb-the-cost-of-indexes/) - 2020-08-13 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-the-cost-of-indexes.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-the-cost-of-indexes.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike Oracle's optimizer-chosen access paths, a DynamoDB secondary index is only used when the application explicitly queries it, and every table write still pays the cost of maintaining each index entry.
- [Amazon DynamoDB Local: running NoSQL on SQLite](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-dynamodb-local/) - 2020-08-07 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB, SQLite | Query Optimization, Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-local.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-dynamodb-local.json)
  Evidence note: DynamoDB Local, a downloadable offline version sharing the cloud service's API, lacks partitioning and appears to use a different storage format entirely, so it cannot be used to measure real performance.
- [RDBMS (vs. NoSQL) scales the algorithm before the hardware](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/rdbms-scales-the-algorithm/) - 2020-08-03 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/rdbms-scales-the-algorithm.json](dbi-services/articles/rdbms-scales-the-algorithm.json)
  Evidence note: Comparing a hash-partitioned DynamoDB access pattern against an Oracle nested-loop index join on up to 2000 keys shows both scale predictably as matched rows per key grow, unlike the claim that RDBMS joins don't scale.
- [A lesson from NoSQL (vs. RDBMS): listen to your users](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/a-lesson-from-nosql-vs-rdbms-listen-to-your-users/) - 2020-08-01 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/a-lesson-from-nosql-vs-rdbms-listen-to-your-users.json](dbi-services/articles/a-lesson-from-nosql-vs-rdbms-listen-to-your-users.json)
  Evidence note: SQL, originally designed per Chamberlin's history as an accessible end-user query language, is now mostly generated by BI tools and ORM frameworks, which often produce suboptimal queries blamed on SQL itself.
- [The myth of NoSQL (vs. RDBMS) “a simpler API to bound resources”](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/myth-nosql-vs-rdbms-relational-queries-are-unbounded/) - 2020-07-21 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Amazon DynamoDB | Query Optimization, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/myth-nosql-vs-rdbms-relational-queries-are-unbounded.json](dbi-services/articles/myth-nosql-vs-rdbms-relational-queries-are-unbounded.json)
  Evidence note: On Oracle's free Autonomous tier, Resource Manager caps CPU and I/O per service even for a SALES join to PRODUCTS, showing a relational call can be bounded like a NoSQL lookup.
- [A Serverless Standby Database called Oracle Autonomous Data Guard](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/a-serverless-standby-database-called-oracle-autonomous-data-guard/) - 2020-07-16 | dbi services | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/a-serverless-standby-database-called-oracle-autonomous-data-guard.json](dbi-services/articles/a-serverless-standby-database-called-oracle-autonomous-data-guard.json)
  Evidence note: Autonomous Data Guard replicates physical redo-applied datafile blocks to its standby rather than logical SQL statements, despite the announcement describing this software-level apply as 'logical changes'.
- [DBPod – le podcast Bases de Données](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dbpod/) - 2020-07-14 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dbpod.json](dbi-services/articles/dbpod.json)
  Evidence note: The DBPod podcast launched with three Oracle-focused episodes covering database versioning between 19c and 20c, Release Updates, and the Multitenant option, distributed through Anchor, Spotify, and Apple Podcasts.
- [19c: scalable Top-N queries without further hints to the query planner](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/19c-scalable-top-n-queries/) - 2020-07-14 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/19c-scalable-top-n-queries.json](dbi-services/articles/19c-scalable-top-n-queries.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle 19c fixes bug 22174392 so a query using FETCH FIRST n ROWS ONLY gets correct low cardinality row estimates without needing the FIRST_ROWS() hint that was previously required in 12c.
- [The myth of NoSQL (vs. RDBMS) “joins dont scale”](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-joins-dont-scale/) - 2020-07-05 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-joins-dont-scale.json](dbi-services/articles/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-joins-dont-scale.json)
  Evidence note: A query joining roughly 900,000 SALES rows to PRODUCTS on Oracle Autonomous Database returns in 92 milliseconds via a nested loop with index range scan, contradicting the claim that relational joins scale poorly.
- [Oracle ACFS: “du” vs. “df” and “acfsutil info”](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-acfs-du-df/) - 2020-07-03 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-acfs-du-df.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-acfs-du-df.json)
  Evidence note: Taking an Oracle ACFS snapshot means any block later modified in the parent or a child volume consumes additional storage, so the free space shown by 'df' can diverge from what a plain 'du' on the mount suggests.
- [Oracle Autonomous Linux: cron’d ksplice and yum updates](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/al7/) - 2020-06-30 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/al7.json](dbi-services/articles/al7.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle Autonomous Linux applies Ksplice kernel updates online without a reboot, configured here on an Always Free OCI compute instance through an API key generated with openssl and the al-config CLI tool.
- [The Oracle ACE program ♠ what it is not ♠](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-ace-program/) - 2020-06-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Indexing, Migration & Compatibility, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-ace-program.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-ace-program.json)
  Evidence note: The Oracle ACE program tracks blog posts, talks, and forum activity through an accumulating points system entered in an APEX application, used to determine eligibility for a level upgrade rather than to rank members.
- [Some myths about PostgreSQL vs. Oracle](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/some-myths-about-postgresql-vs-oracle/) - 2020-06-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Migration & Compatibility; archive: [dbi-services/articles/some-myths-about-postgresql-vs-oracle.json](dbi-services/articles/some-myths-about-postgresql-vs-oracle.json)
  Evidence note: Contrary to a migration comparison guide, Oracle's closest equivalent to PostgreSQL foreign data wrappers is Database Gateway and Heterogeneous Services, and materialized views handle small reference-table distribution.
- [Oracle non-linguistic varchar2 columns to order by without sorting](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/column-level-collate/) - 2020-06-16 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Indexing, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/column-level-collate.json](dbi-services/articles/column-level-collate.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle 12.2's column-level COLLATE clause lets a VARCHAR2 primary key built from mixed letters and numbers sort in plain binary ASCII order through its index even when a linguistic NLS_SORT is set for the session.
- [No{Join,GroupBy}SQL – Analytic Views for BI](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-12c-analytic-views/) - 2020-06-14 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Query Optimization, Internals & Storage, Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-analytic-views.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-analytic-views.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's Analytic Views hide multi-table joins and GROUP BY aggregation behind a single queryable object, giving BI users drill-down access to a snowflake schema hierarchy without writing explicit SQL joins.
- [Oracle 12c – reorg and split table with clustering](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-12c-clustering/) - 2020-06-10 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Indexing, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-clustering.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-clustering.json)
  Evidence note: Using 12c's online ALTER TABLE MERGE PARTITIONS and online partition move together, a time-ordered ingest table can be periodically reorganized to cluster old rows by country instead of by arrival date.
- [Oracle 12c – global partial index](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-12c-global-partial-index/) - 2020-06-10 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Indexing; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-global-partial-index.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-12c-global-partial-index.json)
  Evidence note: A global partial index on COVID-19 case data lets Oracle skip index maintenance for the newest, frequently-loaded partition while still indexing older partitions that were reorganized and clustered by country.
- [Oracle 12c – peak detection with MATCH_RECOGNIZE](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-row-pattern/) - 2020-06-08 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-row-pattern.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-row-pattern.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's MATCH_RECOGNIZE row pattern matching detects peaks directly in a COVID-19 daily case time series using SQL, replacing what would otherwise be a manual visual scan of an Excel line chart.
- [What is a serverless database?](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/what-is-a-serverless-database/) - 2020-06-05 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/what-is-a-serverless-database.json](dbi-services/articles/what-is-a-serverless-database.json)
  Evidence note: A serverless database means server provisioning no longer sits on a project's critical path, contrasted here with a 1996 setup where obtaining and racking Oracle Database hardware took weeks.
- [Oracle 12c – pre-built join index](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-index-on-joins/) - 2020-06-05 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-index-on-joins.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-index-on-joins.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle's Synchronous Refresh for Materialized Views, using REFRESH FAST ON STATEMENT, behaves like a pre-built join index by storing a dimension table's country_code alongside a fact table row automatically.
- [Oracle 18c – select from a flat file](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-select-from-file/) - 2020-06-04 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-select-from-file.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-select-from-file.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle 18c's Inline External Tables let SQLcl query a downloaded COVID-19 case-count CSV file directly with a SELECT statement, without first running SQL*Loader or creating a persistent external table object.
- [티베로 – The AWR-like “Tibero Performance Repository”](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/tibero-ii/) - 2020-05-26 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Backup & Recovery, Migration & Compatibility; archive: [dbi-services/articles/tibero-ii.json](dbi-services/articles/tibero-ii.json)
  Evidence note: Tibero's TPR (Tibero Performance Repository), tested here by running Kevin Closson's SLOB benchmark adapted from Oracle syntax, mirrors Oracle AWR reporting but is driven through the tbsql client instead of sqlplus.
- [Oracle Standard Edition on AWS ☁ socket arithmetic](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-standard-edition-on-aws-a-socket-arithmetic/) - 2020-05-20 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-standard-edition-on-aws-a-socket-arithmetic.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-standard-edition-on-aws-a-socket-arithmetic.json)
  Evidence note: As of May 2020, Oracle Standard Edition 2 licensing on AWS counts every 4 vCPUs as one socket and requires at least 2 sockets per server regardless of whether hyper-threading is enabled.
- [Always free / always up tmux in the Oracle Cloud with KSplice updates](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/always-free-always-up-tmux-in-the-oracle-cloud-with-ksplice-updates/) - 2020-05-14 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/always-free-always-up-tmux-in-the-oracle-cloud-with-ksplice-updates.json](dbi-services/articles/always-free-always-up-tmux-in-the-oracle-cloud-with-ksplice-updates.json)
  Evidence note: Comparing always-free compute tiers, only the Oracle Cloud free VM combines unlimited duration with full SSH access and a public IP, unlike Google Cloud Shell's no-SSH access or AWS's one-year time limit.
- [20c: AWR now stores explain plan predicates](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/20c-awr-now-stores-explain-plan-predicates/) - 2020-05-13 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/20c-awr-now-stores-explain-plan-predicates.json](dbi-services/articles/20c-awr-now-stores-explain-plan-predicates.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle 20c's AWR finally stores filter and access predicates for colored SQL statements, letting DBMS_XPLAN.DISPLAY_AWR show them after bugs previously blocked Statspack and AWR from capturing predicates.
- [The myth of NoSQL (vs. RDBMS) agility: adding attributes](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-agility-adding-attributes/) - 2020-05-07 | dbi services | Database agnostic | Data Modeling & SQL, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-agility-adding-attributes.json](dbi-services/articles/the-myth-of-nosql-vs-rdbms-agility-adding-attributes.json)
  Evidence note: Adding a nullable column to an existing Oracle table only updates data dictionary metadata rather than every existing row, refuting the claim that adding an attribute is costlier in SQL than in a NoSQL JSON document.
- [AWS Aurora IO:XactSync is not a PostgreSQL wait event](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/aws-aurora-xactsync-batch-commit/) - 2020-05-01 | dbi services | PostgreSQL, Amazon Aurora | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/aws-aurora-xactsync-batch-commit.json](dbi-services/articles/aws-aurora-xactsync-batch-commit.json)
  Evidence note: Aurora's IO:XactSync wait event, unlike a plain PostgreSQL commit-time WAL fsync wait, actually reflects Aurora's proprietary distributed storage layer write-durability protocol rather than a local disk sync.
- [티베로 – The most compatible alternative to Oracle Database](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/tibero-i/) - 2020-04-22 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Migration & Compatibility; archive: [dbi-services/articles/tibero-i.json](dbi-services/articles/tibero-i.json)
  Evidence note: TmaxSoft's Tibero database claims deep Oracle SQL and PL/SQL compatibility, evaluated here through a 30-day trial license whose activation key is tied to the output of 'uname -n' on the host running it.
- [Oracle Support: Easy export of SQL Testcase](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-support-easy-export-of-sql-testcase/) - 2020-04-20 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-support-easy-export-of-sql-testcase.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-support-easy-export-of-sql-testcase.json)
  Evidence note: DBMS_SQLDIAG.EXPORT_SQL_TESTCASE builds a complete reproducible test case, including a README and object dump, from just a SQL_ID pulled out of DBA_HIST_SQLTEXT, without manually gathering tables, stats, or parameters.
- [“Segment Maintenance Online Compress” feature usage](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/segment-maintenance-online-compress/) - 2020-04-19 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Operations & Observability; archive: [dbi-services/articles/segment-maintenance-online-compress.json](dbi-services/articles/segment-maintenance-online-compress.json)
  Evidence note: An ALTER TABLE MOVE PARTITION with the ONLINE keyword on a basic-compressed partition sets a distinct online-compression flag bit in TABPART$, which alone triggers the Advanced Compression feature-usage counter.
- [Find the SQL Plan Baseline for a plan operation](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/find-the-sql-plan-baseline-for-a-plan-operation/) - 2020-04-17 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Indexing, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/find-the-sql-plan-baseline-for-a-plan-operation.json](dbi-services/articles/find-the-sql-plan-baseline-for-a-plan-operation.json)
  Evidence note: After a new index makes the optimizer prefer a different plan, that plan loads into the SQL Plan Baseline store as unaccepted, so DBA_SQL_PLAN_BASELINES must be searched by SQL_HANDLE to evolve or drop it.
- [DynamoDB: adding a Local covering index to reduce the cost](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-covering-lsi/) - 2020-03-16 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Cloud & Infrastructure, JSON & Documents; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-covering-lsi.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-covering-lsi.json)
  Evidence note: DynamoDB forces a Local Secondary Index to be declared when the table is created, so turning a partition-key-prefixed covering index into an LSI means rebuilding the table.
- [DynamoDB: adding a Global covering index to reduce the cost](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/dynamodb-covering-gsi/) - 2020-03-16 | dbi services | Amazon DynamoDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Data Modeling & SQL; archive: [dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-covering-gsi.json](dbi-services/articles/dynamodb-covering-gsi.json)
  Evidence note: Even when a DynamoDB table's primary key already filters items, adding a Global Secondary Index with a smaller projected attribute set reduces read cost for queries that only need that narrower attribute set.
- [ysql_bench: the YugaByteDB version of pgbench](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/ysql_bench/) - 2020-03-09 | dbi services | PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB, CockroachDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Transactions & Consistency; archive: [dbi-services/articles/ysql_bench.json](dbi-services/articles/ysql_bench.json)
  Evidence note: YugabyteDB's optimistic concurrency control triggers more serialization retries than PostgreSQL's pgbench natively handles, so a PL/pgSQL retry-wrapped procedure substitutes for pgbench's built-in simple-update script.
- [YugaByteDB 2.1: the Open Source multi-region distributed database with PostgreSQL API is in GA with huge performance improvement](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/yugabytedb-2-1/) - 2020-03-08 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Indexing, Performance & Benchmarking, High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/yugabytedb-2-1.json](dbi-services/articles/yugabytedb-2-1.json)
  Evidence note: YugabyteDB 2.1 GA fixed the slow pgbench initialization time seen in an earlier 1.3 beta and delivered roughly nine times higher pgbench throughput on the same 3-node, replication-factor-3 cluster.
- [Refactoring procedural to SQL – an example with MySQL Sakila](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/refactoring-procedural-to-sql-an-example-with-mysql-sakila/) - 2020-03-01 | dbi services | Oracle Database, MySQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability, Data Modeling & SQL, Events & Community; archive: [dbi-services/articles/refactoring-procedural-to-sql-an-example-with-mysql-sakila.json](dbi-services/articles/refactoring-procedural-to-sql-an-example-with-mysql-sakila.json)
  Evidence note: Refactoring the MySQL Sakila sample schema's rental logic shows translating procedural business rules into declarative SQL, instead of leaving them in stored-procedure loops, improves both performance and readability.
- [Oracle 20c SQL Macros: a scalar example to join agility and performance](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/oracle-20c-sql-macros-a-scalar-example-to-join-agility-and-performance/) - 2020-02-24 | dbi services | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/oracle-20c-sql-macros-a-scalar-example-to-join-agility-and-performance.json](dbi-services/articles/oracle-20c-sql-macros-a-scalar-example-to-join-agility-and-performance.json)
  Evidence note: Oracle 20c scalar SQL Macros let a name-formatting function be inlined by the optimizer at compile time like a C preprocessor macro, avoiding the per-row SQL-to-PL/SQL context switch of a normal function call.
- [SQLNET.EXPIRE_TIME and ENABLE=BROKEN](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/sqlnet-expire_time-and-enablebroken/) - 2020-02-15 | dbi services | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication; archive: [dbi-services/articles/sqlnet-expire_time-and-enablebroken.json](dbi-services/articles/sqlnet-expire_time-and-enablebroken.json)
  Evidence note: Since Oracle Net 12c, server-side Dead Connection Detection relies on TCP keep-alive instead of a TNS packet, and tracing connect() calls on both listener and client confirms when ENABLE=BROKEN is actually still needed.
- [How SQL Server MVCC compares to Oracle and PostgreSQL](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/how-sql-server-mvcc-compares-to-oracle-and-postgresql/) - 2020-02-09 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server | Indexing, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/how-sql-server-mvcc-compares-to-oracle-and-postgresql.json](dbi-services/articles/how-sql-server-mvcc-compares-to-oracle-and-postgresql.json)
  Evidence note: With READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT enabled, SQL Server 2019 still blocks a reader on an indexed column being updated, unlike PostgreSQL and Oracle where MVCC readers never wait on a writer's row lock.
- [Running SQL Server on the Oracle Free tier](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/running-sql-server-on-the-oracle-free-tier/) - 2020-02-08 | dbi services | Oracle Database, Microsoft SQL Server | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [dbi-services/articles/running-sql-server-on-the-oracle-free-tier.json](dbi-services/articles/running-sql-server-on-the-oracle-free-tier.json)
  Evidence note: SQL Server's container binary refuses to start below 2000MB because sqlservr hardcodes that RAM check, so a sub-1GB Oracle Cloud free VM needs sysinfo() interception to fake memory.
- [ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT;](https://www.dbi-services.com/blog/rollback-to-savepoint/) - 2020-02-04 | dbi services | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, Microsoft SQL Server, Db2 | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [dbi-services/articles/rollback-to-savepoint.json](dbi-services/articles/rollback-to-savepoint.json)
  Evidence note: Unlike Oracle, DB2, SQL Server, and MySQL, PostgreSQL discards an entire transaction after any statement error, including a prior successful insert, unless an explicit SAVEPOINT was set before the failing statement.

### 2019

- [Find the best place to work](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/find-best-place-work-franck-pachot) - 2019-11-07 | LinkedIn | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [linkedin/articles/find-best-place-work.json](linkedin/articles/find-best-place-work.json)
  Evidence note: Offers consulting-career guidance centered on choosing employers that build durable technical practices, reusable knowledge, and professional growth rather than optimizing only for immediate billable work.
- [Oracle & Postgres JDBC Fetch Size](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-postgres-jdbc-fetch-size-3012d494712) - 2019-07-26 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-26_Oracle---Postgres-JDBC-Fetch-Size-3012d494712.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-26_Oracle---Postgres-JDBC-Fetch-Size-3012d494712.html)
  Evidence note: Runs identical Java JDBC code against Oracle and PostgreSQL to show that PostgreSQL's ResultSet buffers the entire query result client-side by default instead of using a server cursor.
- [Running pgBench on YugaByteDB 1.3](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/running-pgbench-on-yugabytedb-1-3-3a15450dfa42) - 2019-07-21 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL, YugabyteDB | Performance & Benchmarking, Distributed Databases, Migration & Compatibility, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-21_Running-pgBench-on-YugaByteDB-1-3-3a15450dfa42.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-21_Running-pgBench-on-YugaByteDB-1-3-3a15450dfa42.html)
  Evidence note: Installs YugaByteDB 1.3 as a 3-node single-VM cluster and runs pgbench against its still-beta YSQL layer purely to explore distributed-database behavior rather than measure performance.
- [Oracle DBA_SQL_PLAN_BASELINE SQL_ID and PLAN_HASH_VALUE](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-dba-sql-plan-baseline-sql-id-and-plan-hash-value-8ffa811a7c68) - 2019-07-20 | Medium | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking, Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-20_Oracle-DBA-SQL-PLAN-BASELINE-SQL-ID-and-PLAN-HASH-VALUE-8ffa811a7c68.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-20_Oracle-DBA-SQL-PLAN-BASELINE-SQL-ID-and-PLAN-HASH-VALUE-8ffa811a7c68.html)
  Evidence note: Derives SQL_ID from SQL text with dbms_sqltune_util0.sqltext_to_sqlid and extracts PLAN_HASH_VALUE from dbms_xplan since DBA_SQL_PLAN_BASELINES omits both columns from its view.
- [Passwordless Data Pump 19c](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/passwordless-data-pump-19c-b21cd1e00c16) - 2019-07-16 | Medium | Oracle Database | Security; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-16_Passwordless-Data-Pump-19c-b21cd1e00c16.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-16_Passwordless-Data-Pump-19c-b21cd1e00c16.html)
  Evidence note: Notes that 19c Data Pump still prompts for a password even with a wallet-based passwordless connection configured, but any text entered is ignored since the wallet credential is used.
- [No risk to activate Active Data Guard by mistake with SQL Developer SQLcl](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/no-risk-to-activate-active-data-guard-by-mistake-with-sql-developer-sqlcl-84f285ebdb60) - 2019-07-16 | Medium | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Cloud & Infrastructure, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-16_No-risk-to-activate-Active-Data-Guard-by-mistake-with-SQL-Developer-SQLcl-84f285ebdb60.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-16_No-risk-to-activate-Active-Data-Guard-by-mistake-with-SQL-Developer-SQLcl-84f285ebdb60.html)
  Evidence note: Shows that a plain startup in sqlplus can accidentally open a physical standby read-only with apply, triggering Active Data Guard licensing, while SQLcl instead stopped in mount state.
- [Ideas for Event Sourcing in Oracle](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/ideas-for-event-sourcing-in-oracle-d4e016e90af6) - 2019-07-15 | Medium | Oracle Database | High Availability & Replication, Distributed Databases, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Security; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-15_Ideas-for-Event-Sourcing-in-Oracle-d4e016e90af6.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-15_Ideas-for-Event-Sourcing-in-Oracle-d4e016e90af6.html)
  Evidence note: Surveys XStreams, LogMiner, and the Debezium DBZ-137 connector as change-data-capture options for auditing modifications after Oracle deprecated Streams and Advanced Replication.
- [Oracle Heterogeneous Services](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-heterogeneous-services-ccd5baf85bf7) - 2019-07-12 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-12_Oracle-Heterogeneous-Services-ccd5baf85bf7.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-12_Oracle-Heterogeneous-Services-ccd5baf85bf7.html)
  Evidence note: Revisits Oracle's 1995-era ODBC Gateway, still free in Standard Edition, to run DML, procedures, and transactions across heterogeneous database links from a single Oracle session.
- [Hi Tuyen, as this removes lot of files from the Oracle Home, many features will not work.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/hi-tuyen-as-this-removes-lot-of-files-from-the-oracle-home-many-features-will-not-work-2f17c98bd6db) - 2019-07-09 | Medium | Oracle Database | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-09_Hi-Tuyen--as-this-removes-lot-of-files-from-the-Oracle-Home--many-features-will-not-work--2f17c98bd6db.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-09_Hi-Tuyen--as-this-removes-lot-of-files-from-the-Oracle-Home--many-features-will-not-work--2f17c98bd6db.html)
  Evidence note: Warns that stripping files from the Oracle Home for a minimal footprint leaves only the basic binaries functional, so many optional database features stop working entirely.
- [strace -k (build with libunwind)](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/strace-k-build-with-libunwind-f949d4802322) - 2019-07-05 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-05_strace--k--build-with-libunwind--f949d4802322.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-05_strace--k--build-with-libunwind--f949d4802322.html)
  Evidence note: Rebuilds strace with libunwind support on OEL7 to enable the -k flag, printing full C stack traces for each system call, tested by tracing the Oracle log writer process.
- [Oracle Refresh Group consistency with nested materialized views.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-refresh-group-consistency-with-nested-materialized-views-5466917c490f) - 2019-07-02 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency, Backup & Recovery; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-07-02_Oracle-Refresh-Group-consistency-with-nested-materialized-views--5466917c490f.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-07-02_Oracle-Refresh-Group-consistency-with-nested-materialized-views--5466917c490f.html)
  Evidence note: Tests whether a refresh group keeps two dependent materialized views transactionally consistent with each other, not just each with its own master table, using a constantly updated table.
- [Hi Vivek, Right, but in a mixed workload, reporting is done at the same time as OLTP.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/hi-vivek-right-but-in-a-mixed-workload-reporting-is-done-at-the-same-time-as-oltp-5033ca6b8e4) - 2019-06-28 | Medium | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-28_Hi-Vivek--Right--but-in-a-mixed-workload--reporting-is-done-at-the-same-time-as-OLTP--5033ca6b8e4.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-28_Hi-Vivek--Right--but-in-a-mixed-workload--reporting-is-done-at-the-same-time-as-OLTP--5033ca6b8e4.html)
  Evidence note: Clarifies that Parallel DML, unlike Parallel Query, blocks all modifications on its target tables and any tables linked by referential integrity, making it unsuitable during concurrent OLTP.
- [Hi Gg,](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/hi-gg-b60793913a4e) - 2019-06-28 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-28_Hi-Gg--b60793913a4e.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-28_Hi-Gg--b60793913a4e.html)
  Evidence note: Cites an Oracle support note explaining that parallel execution processes each run their own transaction coordinated by two-phase commit, so concurrent transactions can conflict.
- [Oracle Materialized View Refresh Group atomicity— How to prove transactional consistency with…](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-materialized-view-refresh-group-atomicity-how-to-prove-transactional-consistency-with-e991199d3d24) - 2019-06-26 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-26_Oracle-Materialized-View-Refresh-Group-atomicity--How-to-prove-transactional-consistency-with--e991199d3d24.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-26_Oracle-Materialized-View-Refresh-Group-atomicity--How-to-prove-transactional-consistency-with--e991199d3d24.html)
  Evidence note: Uses LogMiner to read redo and prove, after SQL_TRACE and XCTEND analysis were disputed as inconclusive, that a materialized view refresh group was not refreshed atomically as documented.
- [Oracle ATP: MEDIUM and HIGH services are not for OLTP](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-atp-medium-and-high-services-are-not-for-oltp-5a9281b68d72) - 2019-06-22 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-22_Oracle-ATP--MEDIUM-and-HIGH-services-are-not-for-OLTP-5a9281b68d72.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-22_Oracle-ATP--MEDIUM-and-HIGH-services-are-not-for-OLTP-5a9281b68d72.html)
  Evidence note: Explains that Autonomous Transaction Processing's MEDIUM and HIGH service names silently force Parallel DML, which takes exclusive table locks and causes enq: TM contention under OLTP.
- [Oracle/Hibernate de-queuing](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/oracle-hibernate-de-queuing-7454432a7738) - 2019-06-20 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-20_Oracle-Hibernate-de-queuing-7454432a7738.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-20_Oracle-Hibernate-de-queuing-7454432a7738.html)
  Evidence note: Implements multi-threaded job-queue dequeuing in Hibernate using SELECT FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED with scroll() and setFetchSize() instead of ROWNUM, matching Oracle's internal queuing pattern.
- [A Jupyter notebook on Google Collab to connect to the Oracle Cloud ATP](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/a-jupyter-notebook-on-google-collab-to-connect-to-the-oracle-cloud-atp-5e88b12282b0) - 2019-06-16 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-16_A-Jupyter-notebook-on-Google-Collab-to-connect-to-the-Oracle-Cloud-ATP-5e88b12282b0.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-16_A-Jupyter-notebook-on-Google-Collab-to-connect-to-the-Oracle-Cloud-ATP-5e88b12282b0.html)
  Evidence note: Connects a Google Colab Jupyter notebook to an Oracle Cloud Autonomous Transaction Processing instance using the client credential wallet and Oracle's YUM-hosted client software.
- [Hibernate for Oracle DBAs](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/hibernate-for-oracle-dbas-d57a8c8c2ca9) - 2019-06-05 | Medium | Oracle Database | Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-05_Hibernate-for-Oracle-DBAs-d57a8c8c2ca9.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-05_Hibernate-for-Oracle-DBAs-d57a8c8c2ca9.html)
  Evidence note: Revives decade-old Hibernate 3 test snippets, run from the command line with a manual CLASSPATH instead of an IDE, to let a DBA understand the SQL an ORM mapping actually generates.
- [When Oracle Statistic Gathering times out.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/when-oracle-statistic-gathering-times-out-4d341be23764) - 2019-06-03 | Medium | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Transactions & Consistency, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-06-03_When-Oracle-Statistic-Gathering-times-out--4d341be23764.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-06-03_When-Oracle-Statistic-Gathering-times-out--4d341be23764.html)
  Evidence note: Runs dbms_auto_task_immediate.gather_optimizer_stats manually, without a maintenance-window timeout, after one oversized table kept getting killed and starving others of fresh statistics.
- [PostgreSQL: measuring query activity(WAL size generated, shared buffer reads, filesystem reads,…)](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/postgresql-measuring-query-activity-wal-size-generated-shared-buffer-reads-filesystem-reads-15d2f9b4ca1f) - 2019-05-27 | Medium | PostgreSQL | High Availability & Replication, Internals & Storage, Backup & Recovery, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-27_PostgreSQL--measuring-query-activity-WAL-size-generated--shared-buffer-reads--filesystem-reads----15d2f9b4ca1f.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-27_PostgreSQL--measuring-query-activity-WAL-size-generated--shared-buffer-reads--filesystem-reads----15d2f9b4ca1f.html)
  Evidence note: Diffs pg_stat_database counters and pg_current_wal_lsn() before and after a workload to quantify shared-buffer reads, WAL bytes generated, and row counts from a single unit test.
- [Generate your Oracle Secure External Password Store wallet from your tnsnames.ora](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/generate-your-oracle-secure-external-password-store-wallet-from-your-tnsnames-ora-2fa7ff16803b) - 2019-05-20 | Medium | Oracle Database | Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-20_Generate-your-Oracle-Secure-External-Password-Store-wallet-from-your-tnsnames-ora-2fa7ff16803b.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-20_Generate-your-Oracle-Secure-External-Password-Store-wallet-from-your-tnsnames-ora-2fa7ff16803b.html)
  Evidence note: Provides a script that parses tnsnames.ora to build a Secure External Password Store wallet, matching shell aliases, and a login.sql so SQLcl connects passwordless to every service.
- [Hi GG, this approach(pathfinder) can be used with GTT.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/gg-this-approach-pathfinder-can-be-used-with-gtt-8187c80d1779) - 2019-05-19 | Medium | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-19_Hi-GG--this-approach-pathfinder--can-be-used-with-GTT--8187c80d1779.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-19_Hi-GG--this-approach-pathfinder--can-be-used-with-GTT--8187c80d1779.html)
  Evidence note: Confirms Mauro Pagano's pathfinder tool can be adapted for global temporary tables by filling the relevant data in script.sql ahead of the tagged statement being analyzed.
- [Do you know what you are measuring with pgbench?](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/do-you-know-what-you-are-measuring-with-pgbench-d8692a33e3d6) - 2019-05-19 | Medium | PostgreSQL | Performance & Benchmarking, Transactions & Consistency, Internals & Storage, Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-19_Do-you-know-what-you-are-measuring-with-pgbench--d8692a33e3d6.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-19_Do-you-know-what-you-are-measuring-with-pgbench--d8692a33e3d6.html)
  Evidence note: Compares pgbench's transaction-per-second figure against Kevin Closson's pgio and Brendan Gregg FlameGraphs from perf record to expose which layer actually drives the measured number.
- [I ‘fixed’ execution plan regression with optimizer_features_enable, what to do next?](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/i-fixed-execution-plan-regression-with-optimizer-features-enable-what-to-do-next-ddb99bb7ba15) - 2019-05-16 | Medium | Oracle Database | Query Optimization, Performance & Benchmarking; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-16_I--fixed--execution-plan-regression-with-optimizer-features-enable--what-to-do-next--ddb99bb7ba15.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-16_I--fixed--execution-plan-regression-with-optimizer-features-enable--what-to-do-next--ddb99bb7ba15.html)
  Evidence note: Uses Mauro Pagano's pathfinder tool to narrow a post-upgrade plan regression fixed with optimizer_features_enable down to a single underscore parameter instead of the broad setting.
- [Did you forget to allocate Huge Pages on your PostgreSQL server?](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/did-you-forget-to-allocate-huge-pages-on-your-postgresql-server-7a97e7727b03) - 2019-05-12 | Medium | Oracle Database, PostgreSQL | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-12_Did-you-forget-to-allocate-Huge-Pages-on-your-PostgreSQL-server--7a97e7727b03.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-12_Did-you-forget-to-allocate-Huge-Pages-on-your-PostgreSQL-server--7a97e7727b03.html)
  Evidence note: Shows via pmap that PostgreSQL's huge_pages=try default silently falls back to small pages when the OS has none allocated, unlike Oracle which tries to maximize huge-page usage.
- [Easy Oracle Cloud wallet location in the JDBC connection string](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/easy-oracle-cloud-wallet-location-in-the-jdbc-connection-string-c782d2011252) - 2019-05-10 | Medium | Oracle Database | Transactions & Consistency, Cloud & Infrastructure, Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-10_Easy-Oracle-Cloud-wallet-location-in-the-JDBC-connection-string-c782d2011252.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-10_Easy-Oracle-Cloud-wallet-location-in-the-JDBC-connection-string-c782d2011252.html)
  Evidence note: Adds the TNS_NAMES parameter directly to a JDBC URL and stores the ATP user's password inside the downloaded cloud wallet, removing the need for a tns_admin Java system property.
- [#VDC19 Voxxed Days CERN 2019](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/vdc19-voxxed-days-cern-2019-b3a198b5b796) - 2019-05-05 | Medium | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure, Events & Community; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-05_-VDC19-Voxxed-Days-CERN-2019-b3a198b5b796.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-05_-VDC19-Voxxed-Days-CERN-2019-b3a198b5b796.html)
  Evidence note: Recaps the first Voxxed Days held at CERN, including a CMS-detector site visit and Uncle Bob's keynote warning that shipping unstable code under deadline pressure is a professional failure.
- [Hi Piotr, Bug 29534218 : WITH THE NEW MULTIHOST EZCONNECT METHOD GET WRONG OUTPUTS is in status 16…](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/hi-piotr-bug-29534218-with-the-new-multihost-ezconnect-method-get-wrong-outputs-is-in-status-16-38470abf0bae) - 2019-05-04 | Medium | Database agnostic | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-05-04_Hi-Piotr--Bug-29534218---WITH-THE-NEW-MULTIHOST-EZCONNECT-METHOD-GET-WRONG-OUTPUTS-is-in-status-16--38470abf0bae.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-05-04_Hi-Piotr--Bug-29534218---WITH-THE-NEW-MULTIHOST-EZCONNECT-METHOD-GET-WRONG-OUTPUTS-is-in-status-16--38470abf0bae.html)
  Evidence note: Reports that Oracle bug 29534218, covering wrong outputs from the new multihost EZCONNECT method, sits at status 16, meaning bug screening and triage rather than a confirmed fix.
- [PostgreSQL and Jupyter notebook](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/postgresql-and-jupyter-notebook-e7b68cb6427d) - 2019-04-28 | Medium | PostgreSQL | Operations & Observability; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-04-28_PostgreSQL-and-Jupyter-notebook-e7b68cb6427d.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-04-28_PostgreSQL-and-Jupyter-notebook-e7b68cb6427d.html)
  Evidence note: Installs Anaconda with ipython-sql and a local PostgreSQL server on Windows 10 to run percent-sql magic commands directly inside a Jupyter notebook cell.
- [Avoid compound hints for better Hint Reporting in 19c](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/avoid-compound-hints-for-better-hint-reporting-in-19c-79702f06c735) - 2019-04-26 | Medium | Oracle Database | Query Optimization; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-04-26_Avoid-compound-hints-for-better-Hint-Reporting-in-19c-79702f06c735.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-04-26_Avoid-compound-hints-for-better-Hint-Reporting-in-19c-79702f06c735.html)
  Evidence note: Demonstrates with dbms_xplan's hint-report format that a single USE_NL(A B) hint is really two separate per-table hints, one of which can silently go unused without any warning.
- [Thanks for your feedback Rafael.](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/thanks-for-your-feedback-raphael-3183ad2b4584) - 2019-04-23 | Medium | Oracle Database | General Database; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-04-23_Thanks-for-your-feedback-Rafael--3183ad2b4584.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-04-23_Thanks-for-your-feedback-Rafael--3183ad2b4584.html)
  Evidence note: Notes that a given JDBC driver behavior likely applies from 12c onward but should still be tested on older database versions because of possible driver-side regressions.
- [You should set OCSID.CLIENTID](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/you-should-set-ocsid-clientid-e00cb81ed7e2) - 2019-04-22 | Medium | Oracle Database | Performance & Benchmarking, Operations & Observability, Development & Drivers; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-04-22_You-should-set-OCSID-CLIENTID-e00cb81ed7e2.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-04-22_You-should-set-OCSID-CLIENTID-e00cb81ed7e2.html)
  Evidence note: Sets OCSID.MODULE, OCSID.ACTION, and OCSID.CLIENTID through JDBC's setClientInfo when checking out a pooled Oracle connection so V$SESSION and database links expose the real end user.
- [19c EM Express (aka Oracle Cloud Database Express)](https://medium.com/@FranckPachot/19c-em-express-aka-oracle-cloud-database-express-dfd666911bed) - 2019-04-07 | Medium | Oracle Database | Cloud & Infrastructure; archive: [2018-medium/posts/2019-04-07_19c-EM-Express--aka-Oracle-Cloud-Database-Express--dfd666911bed.html](2018-medium/posts/2019-04-07_19c-EM-Express--aka-Oracle-Cloud-Database-Express--dfd666911bed.html)
  Evidence note: Traces the renaming history from SQL*DBA and OEM Database Control through EM Express to 19c's Oracle Cloud Database Express, then sets its HTTPS port with dbms_xdb_config.
